# MAJOR FINDINGS

## MAJOR-R2-1 — The MAJOR-R1-1 fix's `flex-wrap: wrap` wraps EVERY dispatch row from one line to two, re-introducing the exact `#62` "crop rows mid-line" hazard the same CSS rule's own comment documents — and the new comment asserting the opposite is a confident falsehood

`board/web/css/board.css:623` adds `flex-wrap: wrap` to `.solari-row` (absent at `256d8d5` and at `b865cba` — confirmed by `git show 256d8d5:board/web/css/board.css` and `git show b865cba:...`, both showing `.solari-row { display: flex; gap: 0.6rem; ... }` with no `flex-wrap`). The commit's own comment at `board.css:616-622` claims:

&gt; *"flex-wrap:wrap lets ONLY the nested .solari-superseded-disclosure (flex-basis:100% above) drop to a second line WITHIN this row's own box - **the subject/state/elapsed cluster below stays one line per row (mockup convention preserved**; see the comment on white-space:nowrap immediately below), because none of those three carry flex-basis:100% and .solari-subject's own ellipsis truncation still applies before any of them would wrap."*

Repeated at `board.css:383-384` ("adding `flex-wrap:wrap` here only lets the DISCLOSURE (not the subject/state/elapsed cluster) drop to a second line") and at `board.css:380` ("preserving #67's density argument") and `dom-writer.js:124-125` ("This preserves #67's density").

**Measured, real Chromium, real `board/server` Go binary, real scratch store, 1600×1000 viewport. 18 dispatch rows, NONE carrying a superseded entry** — so the disclosure itself cannot be blamed:

```
===== AS SHIPPED (2861bfd CSS) =====
  distinctRowHeights: [51]
  containerClientH: 176   containerScrollH: 210
  croppedMidRowCount: 3   croppedRows: plain-subject-11/12/13 (top=256, h=51, container bottom=273)
  fullyVisibleRows: 15 of 18
===== SAME PAINT, flex-wrap reverted to the base value (nowrap) =====
  distinctRowHeights: [22]
  containerClientH: 96    containerScrollH: 96
  croppedMidRowCount: 0
  fullyVisibleRows: 18 of 18
```

The counterfactual is exact: `flex-wrap` is the **only** property this diff adds to `.solari-row`, and I reverted it at runtime with `page.addStyleTag` in the same paint — no file edit.

Per-child geometry, same probe (3 rows with a superseded entry, 3 without):

```
subject "no-sup-0"  rowH 73  hasDisclosure false  nonDisclosureVisualLines 2
  .solari-subject  top=99  left=292  ..  .solari-state  top=99  left=377
  .solari-elapsed  top=138 left=292   &lt;- SECOND flex line
```

`.solari-elapsed` drops to its own line on rows that have no disclosure at all.

**The falsehood is contradicted by the comment ten lines below it in the same rule.** `board.css:628-635`, pre-existing and untouched by this diff:

&gt; *"One line per row (mockup convention, every variant): a long subject id truncates with an ellipsis rather than wrapping to a second line - **wrapping is what made the #62 grid's fixed max-height crop rows mid-line** (found live during this car's own visual verification, docs/screenshots/2026-07-26-register-check-candidates/)."*

The new comment cites that very comment ("see the comment on white-space:nowrap immediately below") as its evidence of safety. `white-space: nowrap` (`board.css:636`) stops **text** wrapping inside a flex item; it does not stop **flex items** wrapping onto new flex lines. Measured above: `.solari-rows`' `max-height: 11rem` (`board.css:610`) with `overflow-y: auto` now crops 3 rows mid-row where the base cropped none.

**On the REAL committed `artifacts/` store in this worktree** (server default store resolution, read-only): 4 dispatch rows, **`distinctRowHeights: [73]` as shipped vs `[22]` with `flex-wrap` reverted** — a 3.3× row-height regression on the live board, on rows that are the densest surface `#62`/`#67` exist to protect.

**Scope is wider than the defect it fixes.** MAJOR-R1-1 affected only rows *with* a superseded entry. This regression affects **every** dispatch row, including the majority with none.

**The car's own committed evidence shows it.** `docs/screenshots/2026-07-26-view-69-71-candidates/superseded-collapsed-r2.png` (captured at this commit) renders the dispatch row as three stacked lines — `view-69-71-superseded-de…` / `returned` / `▸ 1 superseded`. Round 1's `collapsed-before-click.png` (captured at `256d8d5`, same fixture) renders `view-69-71-su…  returned` **on one line**. The regression is visible in the two images side by side, and the README caption reads the round-2 image as a pass.

**Why 208/208 could not see it:** nothing in `board/web/test/` asserts rendered geometry. The new structural tests (`dom-writer.test.js:711-806`) run against `createMiniDocument()`, a DOM shim with no layout engine, and assert child counts and class names only. `browser-conditions-persistence.test.js` never touches the dispatches lane. This is `#40`'s territory: the guard for the property the owner ruled on (density) does not exist.

`.car-chip`'s `flex-wrap: wrap` (`board.css:403`) is **clean** — measured 23px, 1 visual line, identical with and without the override, because chips are content-sized rather than grid-cell-constrained.

## MAJOR-R2-2 — This commit created a NEW stale line-number citation, in the same file, in the same hunk, in the commit whose sibling change was a lecture about exactly this class

`board/web/js/dom-writer.js:137`:

```js
// Ephemeral also matches this file's own render cycle unforced: `renderBoard` clears
// `root.textContent` on every repaint (line 141), so even a native `open`
```

| | `b865cba` | `256d8d5` (round 1) | `2861bfd` (HEAD) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `root.textContent = ''` at | line 100 | line **141** | line **192** |
| what is at line 141 | — | the clear | `// every \`&lt;details&gt;\` on the web with no persistence wired at all.` (this comment's own text) |

The car read `141` at `256d8d5`, wrote it into a comment, and its own +51-line insertion **in the same hunk of the same commit** (`3fa9b21`) pushed the target to 192. The citation was false the instant it was committed. `renderBoard` is at `dom-writer.js:191`; the clear at `:192`.

This is MAJOR-R1-4's class verbatim — *"this commit's own insertion broke a live citation and left it stale"* — reproduced one round later. Aggravating: the same commit series wrote `board/web/js/lanes.js:16-23` specifically to explain the hazard:

&gt; *"cited by SYMBOL, not line, per #69/#71 fix cycle round 2 MAJOR-R1-4 and docs/contracts/gating-matrix.md:49's own already-landed lesson: **a line-number citation into a file this SAME train's commit still edits drifts on every later insertion**, and the #65 gate does not check in-range shifts."*

The words about the lesson are present; the substance moved. **DRIFTED.**

Invisible to the gate by construction: `scripts/tests/CitationResolverPolicy.Tests.ps1:1-15` parses `file:line` coordinates and its own header declares *"NOT CAUGHT: an in-range shifted citation"* — a bare `(line 141)` with no filename is not a coordinate it looks for at all. Suite stayed 222 passed.

The same claim on the doc surface is **correct**: `docs/contracts/state-ledger.md:161-162` says *"`renderBoard` clears `root.textContent` on every repaint (`dom-writer.js`)"* — by symbol, no line. Only the code comment is false.

## MAJOR-R2-3 — The MINOR-R1-2 wire-tightening claim names `ingest.js`'s validator as the enforcement hop; measured, that hop enforces nothing, and the schema's own `$comment` says why

`board/web/js/dom-writer.js:142-148`:

```js
// #69/#71 fix-cycle round 2 (MINOR-R1-2): the wire schema now constrains
// superseded[] items to {kind: string, at: string} both required
// (schema/yard-snapshot.schema.json's $defs.dispatchSupersededItem), but
// this function stays defense-in-depth rather than trusting that
// constraint alone - ingest.js's validator only runs against a real
// browser fetch of /schema/yard-snapshot.schema.json (network-reachable at
// runtime); any other caller of renderBoard ... is NOT gated by that fetch.
```

The contrast asserts that callers going *through* that fetch **are** gated. They are not. `$defs.dispatchesPayload` and `$defs.trainsPayload` — the only two places the new `$ref` lands (`schema/yard-snapshot.schema.json:127`, `:194`) — are **never `$ref`'d from `$defs.lane.data`**. The schema's own `$comment` at `schema/yard-snapshot.schema.json:99` states it outright:

&gt; *"…**these three $defs are not $ref'd here** because a lane's data shape is chosen by which lane it is, not by one closed schema union."*

**Measured with the real vendored validator** (`board/web/vendor/cfworker-json-schema/index.js`, draft 2020-12, the exact object `validate.js:19` constructs), against the real schema file at `2861bfd`, on a full snapshot document:

```
GOOD item                -&gt; valid=true   errors=0
kind:7 (wrong type)      -&gt; valid=true   errors=0     &lt;-- NOT rejected
missing at               -&gt; valid=true   errors=0     &lt;-- NOT rejected
empty array              -&gt; valid=true   errors=0
superseded omitted       -&gt; valid=true   errors=0
```

`ingest.js:29`'s `validator.validate(payload)` applies **no** constraint to `superseded[]` items, before or after this diff. Only `scripts/tests/WirePayload.Tests.ps1`'s fragment extraction ever exercises the new `$def`.

This is the class the repo already corrected one file over, in this same train's `#67` cycle — `board/web/js/render.js:391-396`: *"CORRECTED (#67 fix cycle round 2 MINOR-R1-1): the WIRE schema itself does NOT enumerate this set … so the closed-set claim belongs to board/fold alone, never to the schema."* Same overclaim, same schema, same file family, one ticket later.

The renderer guard itself is **correct and non-vacuous** (see the ID table); the defect is the permanent comment asserting a wire-level protection that does not exist, on the exact hop the sentence check traverses.

# MINOR FINDINGS

**MINOR-R2-1 — the owner-ruled "COLLAPSED by default" has zero regression pin.** `grep` across `board/web/test/` finds no assertion on the superseded disclosure's `open` attribute in any form (`hasAttribute('open')` / `getAttribute('open')` appear nowhere near the superseded tests; the only hit on `.solari-superseded-disclosure` is `dom-writer.test.js:895`, a `length === 0` check for the all-malformed case). `renderSupersededDisclosure` never sets `open`, and I observed `anyDisclosureOpenByDefault: false` live on the real store — so it is correct today. But a future edit adding `details.setAttribute('open','')` leaves 208/208 green. `board.css:376-377` asserts *"COLLAPSED by default (no `open` attribute is ever set…)"* by reading only. `#40`: unwatched.

**MINOR-R2-2 — the structural-containment witness recorded in the screenshots README is a tautology.** `docs/screenshots/2026-07-26-view-69-71-candidates/README.md:45-46`: *"Structural containment (`disclosure.closest('.solari-row').contains(disclosure)`) was observed `true` live in the browser at capture time."* `Element.closest()` returns an ancestor-or-self, and `contains()` is true for any ancestor — the expression can only evaluate `true` or throw on `null`. It cannot distinguish the fixed code from round 1's sibling layout in any way that matters (under round 1's code `closest` would have returned `null` and thrown, so it is not *entirely* vacuous — but "it threw vs it didn't" is not the containment claim being made). The underlying claim is true; I verified it independently (`disclosureClosestRowIsThisRow: true` and `disclosureIsDescendant: true` for all 13 disclosures). The landed guards in `dom-writer.test.js:747-753` and `:790-796` are properly falsifiable; only the README's recorded witness is not.

# THE WIRE SENTENCE — `$defs.dispatchSupersededItem`, every hop, file:line

1. **Producer type** — `board/fold/fold.go:85-88`: `DispatchSupersededItem{ Kind string \`json:"kind"\`; At string \`json:"at"\` }`. Non-pointer strings, no `omitempty` → both keys always marshal.
2. **Producer population** — `board/fold/algorithm.go:196-201`: `superseded := []DispatchSupersededItem{}`; for each loser, `append(superseded, DispatchSupersededItem{Kind: k, At: at})`. `k`/`at` come from `rec.str(...)`, which yields `""` rather than absence — so a degenerate emission is an empty *string*, never a missing or non-string key. The `$def`'s `required` can therefore never reject a real emission; the schema's own description at `:70` says exactly this and is **accurate**.
3. **Marshal** — `board/fold/output.go:9-18`: `"superseded"` is one of the four unconditional keys; `nil` normalised to `[]`.
4. **Assemble → wire** — `board/assemble/assemble.go:187` `dispatchWireMap(d, …)`, `map[string]any` from `json.Marshal(d)`; carried on `assemble.DispatchesPayload` / `TrainsPayload`.
5. **Schema constraint** — `schema/yard-snapshot.schema.json:69-76` `$defs.dispatchSupersededItem`, `$ref`'d at `:127` (trains cars) and `:194` (dispatches). Correctly **not** applied to `IntentSupersededItem` (which has no `Kind`) — no intent `superseded` exists in this schema.
6. **Client validation — THE BREAK.** `board/web/js/ingest.js:29` → `validate.js:19` `new Validator(schema, '2020-12')`. `$defs.lane.data` (`schema:96-101`) is an unconstrained free object and the payload `$defs` are **not `$ref`'d** from it (`schema:99`, declared). **Measured: `superseded: [{kind: 7}]` validates `valid=true, errors=0`.** The comment at `dom-writer.js:142-148` names this hop as the enforcement locus → **MAJOR-R2-3**.
7. **View model** — `board/web/js/render.js:312` (`superseded: c.superseded || []`) and `:370` (`superseded: d.superseded || []`). Pass-through, no filtering.
8. **Guarded read** — `board/web/js/dom-writer.js:150-152`: `superseded.filter((item) =&gt; typeof item?.kind === 'string' &amp;&amp; typeof item?.at === 'string')`; empty → `return null` (no empty `&lt;details&gt;`). This is where the constraint actually bites at runtime.
9. **DOM** — `dom-writer.js:153-161`: `&lt;details class="…-disclosure"&gt;&lt;summary class="…-summary"&gt;N superseded&lt;/summary&gt;&lt;div class="…"&gt;&lt;span|a class="…-entry"&gt;kind at&lt;/span&gt;&lt;/div&gt;&lt;/details&gt;`, appended to the **row** (`:534`) / **chip** (`:451`).
10. **Hand-maintained mirrors** — `scripts/tests/WirePayload.Tests.ps1:48-51` (the extraction helper, modified this round; verified sound and non-vacuous above). No wire sample or snapshot fixture carries a `superseded` item that the `$def` would newly reject — the WirePayload samples at `:66`, `:141` are the only ones and both pass.
11. **Computed style, expanded, real browser** — entry `tagName "SPAN"`, `href null` (no GitHub env in this probe — the degrade-honest branch), `color rgba(217,213,201,0.55)` = `var(--text-dim)`, `text-decoration-line none`, `border-bottom 0px none`, `entryStillInsideOwnRow: true`, `entryWithinRowBox: true`, text `dispatched 2026-07-20T08:00:00Z` rendered VERBATIM.
12. **Cross-surface isolation, measured** — clicking a superseded `&lt;summary&gt;` writes **nothing** to `#69`'s storage: `sessionStorage` before `null`, after `{key: null, len: 0, allKeys: []}`. `app.js:47-59`'s `handleConditionToggle` returns early on any `&lt;details&gt;` carrying neither `data-conditions-strip` nor `data-condition-code`. No leak.

# THE M1-EPHEMERALITY ADJUDICATION: **PERMITTED**

Ruled explicitly, against each binding source, read first-hand rather than from the brief.

**Issue #69's own text** (`gh issue view 69 --json body`) scopes persistence to one surface: *"the strip is a native details element whose open state lives only in the DOM… Fix: preserve the open attribute across DOM rebuilds…, and **persist across page refreshes (sessionStorage keyed to the strip)**. An operator who opened the flags wants them to STAY open until dismissed - **the alarm detail** is the thing they were reading."* The owner-reported defect is the conditions strip; the rationale is alarm-reading. The superseded disclosure is a different surface that did not exist at filing, and it carries no alarm. **No contradiction.**

**The owner's M1 ruling**, verbatim from issue #69's comment (2026-07-27): *"superseded-record history re-attaches INSIDE its owner row/chip … and renders behind a quiet per-row disclosure, **COLLAPSED by default** - the same details idiom the conditions strip uses. Rationale: matches the #68 principle (not hidden, just not surfaced) and preserves the #67 density the reviewer measured as regressed. Options considered and declined: always-visible nesting (reintroduces the density regression), per-row cap a la #67."* Both stated rationales and both declined options are about **density and surfacing** — never persistence. The ruling names no persistence requirement. **No contradiction.**

I note one ambiguity for the record: *"the same details idiom the conditions strip uses"* is readable as chrome-only or chrome-plus-`sessionStorage`. Under this shop's own rule that a requirement readable two ways will be read both ways, that phrase is a latent hazard — but the brief authorised the car to take the minimal option and flag it, and it flagged it in three places (`dom-writer.js:126-141`, `design.md:783-792`, `state-ledger.md:155-166`). Choosing-and-disclosing is what it was told to do; this is not a finding.

**The state ledger's contracts.** `docs/contracts/state-ledger.md:155-166` declares the disclosure explicitly NOT a ledger field, with reasoning. I verified every claim in that paragraph: no `sessionStorage` key (`grep` + measured: `sessionStorage.length === 0` after a real click), no JS variable, and `renderBoard` does clear `root.textContent` (`dom-writer.js:192` — cited by symbol here, correctly). CLAUDE.md's living-contracts rule requires the ledger be updated in the same commit; it was, honestly, as a declared non-row. **No contradiction.**

**Ruling: PERMITTED.** The car's stated reason is also factually correct — the DOM is rebuilt wholesale, so persistence would require new capture-and-reapply plumbing, not reuse of `#69`'s. The one thing missing is not persistence but a **pin on the collapsed default** (MINOR-R2-1).

# CONVERGENCE RULING — **the swirl trigger FIRES (two of three conditions), cap set at ONE more round**

| Condition | Met? | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Major counts stop declining | **No** | 4 → 3 Major; 3 → 2 Minor. Genuinely declining. |
| Findings cluster in the same section across rounds | **Yes** | Round 1: 1 of 4 Majors in the superseded-rendering surface. Round 2: **3 of 3** — `board.css`'s `.solari-row`/`.car-superseded-disclosure` rules and `dom-writer.js`'s `renderSupersededDisclosure` comment block. |
| A round's findings include defects the previous round's fixes CREATED | **Yes — 2 of 3 Majors** | MAJOR-R2-1 and MAJOR-R2-2 are both created by `3fa9b21`, the MAJOR-R1-1 fix. Neither existed at `256d8d5`. |

Per CLAUDE.md's calibration, two of three fires the escalation. **I set the cap at ONE further fix round.** If a round-3 delta review finds any Major in the superseded-rendering surface again, escalate to the owner rather than dispatching round 4. The conductor honours the cap.

**But I do not think this is a genuine swirl, and the more useful ruling is about the instrument, not the count.** Both rounds inherited the same unquestioned premise: *that a CSS change's rendered effect can be verified by reading the CSS plus looking at one screenshot.* Round 1's MAJOR-R1-1 existed because a one-subject fixture could not falsify the layout claim. Round 2's MAJOR-R2-1 exists because the screenshot that **shows** the regression was read as a pass. Same premise, opposite manifestation, twice — and no gate in the repo can catch either, because nothing under `board/web/test/` observes layout (`createMiniDocument()` has no layout engine; the two real-browser tests never touch the dispatches lane).

Recommended remedy, and it is a probe-doctrine landing rather than more review: **a real-browser test that asserts rendered geometry** — for a dispatch row with no superseded entry, the count of distinct child `top` values must be 1, and `.solari-rows` must have zero rows crossing its own `clientHeight` boundary. That reds by name the instant the "one line per row" mockup convention breaks, which is the property `#62`, `#67` and this ruling all depend on and which three consecutive commits have now been unable to protect by reading. My probe scripts (paths below) are the working recipe.

The fix cycle should go to the **same car** with a delta brief carrying these IDs — MAJOR-R2-1/2/3 are mechanical (a CSS approach that does not set `flex-wrap` on `.solari-row`; two comment corrections), and the car holds the unwritten context.

# MINOR FINDINGS

**MINOR-R3-1 (measured, undisclosed, created by this round's fix) — an EXPANDED superseded entry now wraps to two lines at most desktop widths.** This round moved `white-space: nowrap` off `.solari-row` (where it covered the whole subtree, including the entries) onto `.solari-row-main` (`board.css:636`, now scoped to the cluster only). The entry therefore computes `white-space: normal`. Swept 9 viewports, real Chromium, real server, one expanded entry:

```
vw= 340  cols=285px            entry 256x14  ws=normal ~lines=1  rowH=52
vw= 500  cols=210x2            entry 182x28  ws=normal ~lines=2  rowH=66
vw= 760  cols=219x3            entry 190x28  ws=normal ~lines=2  rowH=66
vw=1024  cols=220x3            entry 191x28  ws=normal ~lines=2  rowH=66
vw=1280  cols=223x4            entry 194x28  ws=normal ~lines=2  rowH=66
vw=1400  cols=253x4            entry 224x14  ws=normal ~lines=1  rowH=52
vw=1600  cols=237x5            entry 209x28  ws=normal ~lines=2  rowH=66
vw=1920  cols=237x5            entry 209x28  ws=normal ~lines=2  rowH=66
vw=2560  cols=237x5            entry 209x28  ws=normal ~lines=2  rowH=66
```

Six of nine, including 1600px and 1920px. At round 2 the same entry at 1600px measured `218x14` — one line. The cluster itself stays one line at every width (`clusterLines=1` throughout), so `MAJOR-R2-1` is genuinely fixed; this is a neighbouring surface.

**I considered Major and declined, with reasons on the page so the call can be second-guessed:** it contradicts no comment, no owner ruling, and no spec — the "One line per row" mockup convention documented at `board.css:628-635` is explicitly about the subject id cluster, and the car correctly moved that comment along with the rule it describes. Nothing is truncated, hidden, cropped, or mis-attributed; Law 1 is not engaged. It manifests only after a reader deliberately clicks a disclosure open, so HAVAGLANCE's glance is untouched. And the one-line rendering it replaces was incidental inheritance from a rule that this round correctly re-scoped, in a layout that was itself rejected. Calling this a Major would fire an owner escalation over a post-click cosmetic wrap that no authority forbids — the autoimmune mode. **Disposition: a one-line `white-space: nowrap` on `.solari-superseded-entry`, or a ticket.** Also worth recording: **the instrument I prescribed does not cover this** — it asserts the cluster's line count and plain-row cropping, not the entry's. A reviewer-specified instrument guards the property named and not its neighbour.

**MINOR-R3-2 (Law 1, comment vs code).** `board/web/test/browser-dispatch-row-geometry.test.js:16` — *"A NARROW viewport (500px) forces .solari-rows' auto-fill grid to a single column"*. The code at `:41` uses `viewport: { width: 340, height: 1000 }`. Measured: at 340px the grid is one 285px column (single column, as claimed); at 500px it is **two** 210px columns — so the comment's stated width would not even produce the property it claims. Wrong number, and wrong about its own consequence.

**MINOR-R3-3 (measured false, self-contradicted three lines later).** `docs/screenshots/2026-07-26-view-69-71-candidates/README.md`, the disclosed-artifact paragraph: *"structurally impossible to fully avoid in a CSS Grid with auto-sized row tracks and mixed-height siblings"*. Measured false — `align-items: start` on `.solari-rows` fully removes it (see the adjudication below), and the README **itself proposes exactly that** in its next sentence. Say "not worth fixing here" or "avoidable with `align-items: start`, deferred"; not "impossible".

**MINOR-R3-4 (caption inaccurate about the image it describes).** Same README, Round 3 section: *"every plain row and the OTHER disclosure row (`with-sup-1`) keep their original one-line height, unaffected."* Measured: `with-sup-1` is a **38px, two-line** row (19px cluster + 13px collapsed summary), never one line. The substance — *unaffected* — is correct and is what geometry test 3 asserts; the *"one-line"* qualifier is false for the one row it is attached to by name. This is the round-2 lesson in miniature: a caption asserting something the measurement contradicts.

**MINOR-R3-5 (the MAJOR-R2-2 audit is one instance short, and its stated reason is wrong for that instance).** I walked every added line across the whole train (`git diff 256d8d5..4b50a5c`) for line-number citations. Five hits, all verified at HEAD:

| Citation | In | Target file edited by this train? | True at HEAD? |
|---|---|---|---|
| `fold.go:112` | `assemble_test.go` | No | Yes — `\tSpend   any` |
| `gating-matrix.md:49` | `dom-writer.js`, `lanes.js` | No | Yes — the "cited by symbol, not line, per #65's own lesson" row |
| `"(line 141)"` | `dom-writer.js:222` | n/a | Quotation of the removed falsehood inside its own correction — not a live citation |
| **`app.js:148`** | `browser-conditions-persistence.test.js` | **YES — `+39/-1` at `256d8d5`** | Yes — `root.addEventListener('toggle', handleConditionToggle, true);` |

The citation itself is **true**, and `app.js` has not been touched since `256d8d5` (`git diff 256d8d5..4b50a5c -- board/web/js/app.js` → 0 files). But the audit's stated justification — *"in files this train never edits"* — is factually wrong for `app.js`, and its stability is circumstantial rather than structural: any future commit inserting above line 148 drifts it silently, and the #65 gate does not check in-range shifts (`CitationResolverPolicy.Tests.ps1:12`). Bounded: the justification lives only in the car's report, not in any committed surface (`grep` for it across the repo returns nothing), so no durable artifact carries the false claim.

**MINOR-R3-6 (false technical claim in a permanent comment; measured no-op).** `board/web/css/board.css:388-391`: *"`flex-basis:100%` in a COLUMN container sizes the cross-axis dimension (HEIGHT, not width), which would be wrong here."* Two errors: in a column flex container the vertical axis is the **main** axis, not the cross axis; and the asserted consequence does not occur. Measured — forcing `flex-basis: 100%` onto `.solari-superseded-disclosure` at 1400px changed **nothing** (`discH` 13 before and after, `rowBoxH` unchanged), because the row's height is content-driven and a percentage basis resolves against an indefinite height. The decision (omit the property) is fine by minimalism; only the reason is wrong. Blast radius zero, which is why this is Minor and not the class MAJOR-R2-3 was.

---

# THE DISCLOSED OUT-OF-SCOPE OBSERVATION — adjudicated

**Is it real?** Yes. Measured at 1400px (4 columns, 9 rows over 3 grid bands):

```
band 1: plain-subject-00..03   rowBoxH 22   mainH 19   mainVisualLines 1
band 2: plain-subject-04,05,06 rowBoxH 38   mainH 19   mainVisualLines 1   &lt;- stretched
        with-sup-0             rowBoxH 38   mainH 19   discH 13
band 3: with-sup-1             rowBoxH 38   mainH 19   discH 13
```

Plain rows measure 38px **only in the band that also holds a disclosure row**. Their content stays one line (`mainH: 19`, one distinct `top`) — the extra 16px is blank box, not wrapped text. Computed `align-items` on `.solari-rows` reads `normal`, which for a grid container behaves as `stretch`.

**Is the README's description accurate?** Yes, on every checkable claim: not content wrapping (confirmed — `mainVisualLines: 1`), caused by grid stretch across a shared row band (confirmed), 38px vs 22px (confirmed exactly), and distinct from `MAJOR-R2-1`'s failure mode (confirmed — no crop, cluster one line, geometry test still green). The one clause that fails is `MINOR-R3-3`'s "impossible to avoid".

**Is it pre-existing?** The **mechanism** predates the entire train: `.solari-rows`' rule is **byte-identical** at `b865cba` and at `4b50a5c` (`display: grid; grid-template-columns: repeat(auto-fill, minmax(13rem, 1fr)); gap: 0.15rem 1.5rem; max-height: 11rem; overflow-y: auto;`) with `align-items` never declared. What is new is the **trigger** — mixed-height siblings — which arrives with `#71`'s feature. The earliest commit where a *disclosure-bearing row* can stretch a plain row is `2861bfd` (round 2), where it was confounded because the wrap regression made every row 73px anyway; `4b50a5c` is the first commit where it is cleanly separable. The README's wording — *"not new to this round's fix … present since MAJOR-R1-1 first shipped round-2"* — is **precisely correct** and does not overclaim pre-existence relative to the train.

**Was not fixing it the right call under the cap?** **Yes.** It is outside all five named findings, it is cosmetic (blank box, no lost or misplaced information, no crop, Law 1 untouched), and a same-round unscoped CSS change to the shared grid rule is exactly the move that produced `MAJOR-R2-1`. Disclosing it and stopping is the correct application of the cap.

**Recommended disposition: TICKET, not live-with, and it is cheap.** Measured, same paint, `align-items: start` on `.solari-rows`: all seven plain rows drop **38px → 22px**, both disclosure rows correctly stay 38px, nothing else moves. One property, fully effective, verified. It wants its own ticket because it touches a shared grid rule that governs the whole dispatches lane and deserves its own geometry assertion — the same lesson this train paid three rounds for.

## ADJUDICATION 1 — the car was RIGHT to deviate. The conductor's premise was FALSE.

**Ruling: the car's honest stop was correct, and the brief's stated precedent does not exist.**

- `board/fold/fold.go:103-125` — `DispatchEntry` is `Subject, State, At, Superseded, winnerKind, Outcome, Spend, ElapsedSeconds, BudgetSeconds, BudgetSource`. **There is no `RecordDir` field.** A grep of `board/fold/*.go` for `recordDir|RecordDir` returns **zero** hits. The conductor's "recordDir is the precedent for that location" is disproved by opening the file.
- recordDir actually lives in `board/assemble`: `recordDirBySubject` at `assemble.go:346`, augmenting the wire map at `assemble.go:247-249`. The car put `taskId` in exactly the same place (`assemble.go:426`, `:250-252`).
- **The car's stated reason is also true.** `schema/vectors/README.md:4-9` — "the landed pwsh detector (`scripts/Detect-Dispatches.ps1`) and the Go port ... conform to THESE fixtures, and the D18 cross-verifier CI job runs BOTH against them, failing on any divergence"; step 3 of the runner contract compares `dispatches` **DEEP-EQUAL**. A Go-only field on `fold.DispatchEntry.MarshalJSON` would have broken every vector's `dispatches` comparison on one side of a two-implementation contract.
- **No leakage.** `git diff --name-only bc4c28f..82f64b5 -- board/fold scripts/` returns **empty**. Vectors still pass in both languages (Go 18/18 subtests; Pester Detector 22/22).

Had the car obeyed the brief, it would have shipped a cross-language contract break. Honest stop = success outcome, and it earned it.

## ADJUDICATION 2 — first-write-wins IS wrong here, and the car's matcher DOES pick the fold winner. Proven by injection.

**Ruling: correct, and stronger than the car argued.**

- `board/store/store.go:193` — `sort.Strings(paths)`. For one subject, `dispatched-*.json` sorts before `returned-*.json` (`d` &lt; `r`), so `recordDirBySubject`'s first-write-wins (`assemble.go:353-355`) would land on the task_id-less dispatched record.
- **I constructed the multi-record case and observed it** (reviewer-owned browser probe, real Go server, scratch store): subject `c0ffee1234567890abcdef0123456789` with `dispatched@09:10`, `returned@09:20 task_id=LOSER-earlier-task-id`, `returned@09:30 task_id=WINNER-later-task-id`. Observed wire: `"taskId":"WINNER-later-task-id"`. The **fold winner**, not first-or-last-scanned.
- **Fault injection at the Go layer**, disclosed below: removing only the `at == d.At` winner pin from `taskIDForReturnedSubject` produced
  `d["taskId"] = solo-1-earlier-task-id, want the WINNING (newer, 11:00) record's task_id "solo-1-winning-task-id"` — the guard fires with the exact wrong value, so it is not vacuous.
- **The one hole I looked for and did not find:** two returned records at the EXACT same `at`. Measured (scratch store, `returned-a.json` REJECT/`A-first-in-path-order`, `returned-b.json` APPROVE/`B-second-in-path-order`): wire renders `"outcome":"REJECT"` **and** `"taskId":"A-first-in-path-order"` — outcome and task-id come from the SAME record the fold chose. Coherent. No finding.

## ADJUDICATION 3 — the enumeration is COMPLETE.

**Ruling: correct.** I walked every identity-label render site in `board/web/js/`:

## THE WIRE SENTENCE — `task_id`, producer to pixel, every hop

1. **Producer, RETURNED only — CONFIRMED.** `scripts/Produce-Artifact.ps1:358` — `if ($Kind -eq 'returned' -and $taskId) { $record['task_id'] = $taskId }`. The `-and $taskId` guard also means an empty/whitespace value is never stamped. Sourced at `:243` from the envelope (`if ($env.PSObject.Properties['TaskId']) { $taskId = $env.TaskId }`), declared optional at `:240-242`. The `else` branch at `:265` is the dispatched path — it stamps `subject_basis`/`model`/`provenance`, **never `task_id`**. Issue #75's probe comment holds.
2. **Store parse.** `board/store/store.go:134` — `TaskID string \`json:"task_id"\`` (landed by #51), so the field is a *declared* key and does not fire `record-unrecognised-fields` (`store.go:310-324`). Content access is the generic decode at `store.go:263`, surfaced as `Record.Fields` (`store.go:308`).
3. **Resolution.** `assemble.go:61` `taskIDs := taskIDsBySubject(in.Records, in.Fold.Dispatches)` → `assemble.go:426` (skip any `d.State != "returned"`) → `assemble.go:450` `taskIDForReturnedSubject` matches `subject` + `kind=="returned"` + `at == the fold's own winning At`. **Single owner** — no second derivation anywhere (Law 6).
4. **Assemble/wire hop, two consumers of that one map.** Trains: `assemble.go:123` `TaskID: taskIDs[m.Subject]` → `types.go:36` `TaskID string \`json:"taskId,omitempty"\``. Dispatches: `assemble.go:193` passes `taskIDs[d.Subject]` into `dispatchWireMap`, which at `assemble.go:250-252` sets `m["taskId"]` **only when non-empty** — key omitted, never `""`.
5. **Schema constraint.** `schema/yard-snapshot.schema.json:131-134` (trains car) and `:207-210` (dispatch entry), both `{"type":"string"}`, both **outside** the unchanged `required` arrays (`["subject","role","state","at"]`, `["subject","state","at","assigned"]`). Absent-form validates unchanged — evidenced by `WirePayload.Tests.ps1` 7/7 and `probe-yard-snapshot.mjs` PASS (`sample-validates: PASS`, non-vacuity `rejected as expected`) against samples that carry no `taskId`.
6. **Client view model, null-not-undefined.** `render.js:320` `taskId: c.taskId ?? null`; `render.js:374` `taskId: d.taskId ?? null`.
7. **DOM render + fallback.** `dom-writer.js:459` `car.taskId ?? car.subject` then `carSubject.setAttribute('title', car.subject)`; `dom-writer.js:552` `d.taskId ?? d.subject` then `:559` `subject.setAttribute('title', d.subject)`.
8. **Provenance link is untouched by the label.** `links.js:16-19` `buildRecordLink(cfg, recordDir)` takes **only** `recordDir`; both call sites still pass `*.recordDir`. Observed, real browser, `STARCAR_GITHUB_REPO=polecatspeaks/StarCar STARCAR_GITHUB_REF=dev`: visible text `reviewer-75-handle-r1`, `title="reviewer75hashwithtaskid"`, `href="https://github.com/polecatspeaks/StarCar/tree/dev/&lt;prefix&gt;/reviewer75hashwithtaskid"`, `tag=A`, `class="solari-subject provenance-link"`. Unset → `tag=SPAN`, `href=null`. Both branches honest.
9. **Hand-maintained mirrors.** `WirePayload.Tests.ps1` carries no `taskId` sample — **I rule this NOT a finding**, and better-supported than the car argued: (a) `#28`'s `recordDir` set the identical precedent and carries no sample either; (b) `schema/yard-snapshot.schema.json` has **no `additionalProperties` anywhere** (grep: zero hits), so an added optional key cannot break any existing sample; (c) `dom-writer.js:150-162`'s own *measured* comment records that `$defs.lane.data` does not `$ref` these payload defs, so the browser's real validator applies **no** constraint to lane data either way. The new field's type constraint is therefore structurally inert at both runtimes; a pwsh sample would exercise nothing the Go `string` type does not already guarantee. NIRTS call: correct.

Evidence, all re-derivable:
- `board/web/verify-registers.mjs:104` — `await captureState(browser, 'calm-yard', {})`, **no `storePath` override**, so `calm-yard.png` is captured against the **ambient real `artifacts/` store**. `:105` `hot-yard` uses `buildScratchStoreWithInFlightDispatch()`, which `cpSync`s that same real store (`real-board-server.js:61`).

- I opened both images. `docs/screenshots/2026-07-26-register-check-candidates/calm-yard.png` and `docs/screenshots/2026-07-26-view-28-12-candidates/calm-yard.png` **both** show a dispatches row rendered `a076bf6c0a94e…`. After this train the identical capture renders `process-42-car-r3`. The images no longer depict what the code draws for the exact surface this train redefines — and it is the exact hash-as-identity complaint issue #75 was filed to remove.
- **The house has a landed, same-class remedy from this same train family, one day old:** `docs/screenshots/2026-07-26-view-69-71-candidates/README.md` carries a `**DISCLOSED STALENESS (fix cycle round 2, MAJOR-R1-1, 2026-07-27):**` block for a rendering its images "also incidentally show." That precedent was created under a Major. `2026-07-26-register-check-candidates/` has **no README at all** in which such a note could live, and the four preceding view trains (#62, #28/#12, #67, #69/#71) each captured their own evidence directory; #75 captured none.

**The counter-argument, stated so the owner can rule cheaply rather than re-derive it:** these are dated per-train evidence captures; the only prose that cites one (`docs/design/mockups/2026-07-26-claude-design-board/README.md:65`, a 1920×1080 responsive-layout claim) remains **true**; and an ambient-store capture is data-stale on every commit that lands a record, which no rule requires recapturing. I still rule Major, because what changed here is the **rendering rule**, not the data — precisely the class the #69/#71 README disclosed rather than dismissed — and because the report's clearance is an affirmative claim over a surface that was never opened. Remedy is cheap: the #69/#71-shaped disclosure note, same commit.

### R1-m1 (minor) — a schema-valid `taskId: ""` renders a BLANK identity label

Measured, real browser, real served modules (`/js/render.js`, `/js/dom-writer.js`):

```
EMPTY-STRING   viewModelTaskId=""              renderedText=""                    title="a103c4067cf9267a3"
ABSENT         viewModelTaskId=null            renderedText="a103c4067cf9267a3"   title="a103c4067cf9267a3"
REAL           viewModelTaskId="view-75-car-r1" renderedText="view-75-car-r1"     title="a103c4067cf9267a3"
```

`schema/yard-snapshot.schema.json:132,208` declare `{"type":"string"}` with no `minLength`, and the schema has no `additionalProperties` gate anywhere, so `""` is wire-valid; `??` at `render.js:374` and `dom-writer.js:552` does not treat `""` as absent. The row loses its visible identity entirely. **Not Major:** unreachable through the only shipped producer (`assemble.go:250` guards `taskID != ""`; `types.go:36` is `omitempty`). Worth fixing because the sibling field's guard at `links.js:17` (`!recordDir`) *is* truthiness-based, so #75 introduced an inconsistency with its own nearest precedent; the fix is one character.

### R1-m2 (minor) — `browser-dispatch-row-taskid.test.js:57` is named for a scenario its fixture does not construct

The test is titled *"a **long** task-id still gets `.solari-subject`'s ellipsis truncation"*, but `buildScratchStoreForTaskIdTitles` (`real-board-server.js:472`) seeds `task_id: 'view-75-car-r1'` — **14 characters**, shorter than the 20-char hash it replaces. Measured in that fixture: `clientWidth 123 / scrollWidth 131` — it overflows by **8px**, by accident of the 13rem grid column, not because the id is long. The assertion itself is length-**independent** (computed `overflow`/`text-overflow` only), so it cannot fail for a length reason. No Major, because I independently measured the genuinely long case clean (76 chars: `scrollWidth 713 / clientWidth 123`, `distinctTopCount 1`, row height unchanged at 38px) — but the name promises coverage the assertion does not deliver, which is the instrument-honesty class this shop guards.

### R1-m3 (nit) — a comment left stale by the commit that invalidated it, 24 lines above the change

`board/web/js/dom-writer.js:528-529` still reads *"DISPATCHES: … dense monospace rows: **subject**, state word, elapsed"*. As of `:552` the first cell is task-id-or-subject. Same-file, same-function, same commit.

**Against the doctrine that landed this morning:** I read `dev`'s CLAUDE.md (this worktree's copy predates it — the branch is based on `bc4c28f`). The ruling requires a README "stating what the images validated, the commit they were captured at, and any known divergence since", with labelling *never* recapture, mechanically floored by DocPolicy's `Status:` gate. **Both READMEs satisfy that shape**: what was validated (§ bullets), capture commit (`45b0e12`/`3076a40`), divergence (the DISCLOSED STALENESS block), `Status: Current` at line 3. I confirmed DocPolicy is non-vacuous here — stripping the Status line from the register-check README made it fail **naming that exact file**. The car's "recapturing would still be dated per-train evidence" reasoning also lands on the right side of the new ruling.

### R1-m1 — fixed, and the second half of the question answered

Fix: `render.js:325` `taskId: c.taskId || null` and `render.js:379` `taskId: d.taskId || null`. **Non-vacuity re-derived:** reverting both to `?? null` reds **exactly and only** the two new pins (`render.test.js`: 47 pass / 2 fail).

**"Did it alter behaviour for any other falsy-but-meaningful value?" — swept through the real served modules, no value lost:**
```
absent  -&gt; null -&gt; "sweepsubject"      zero  -&gt; null -&gt; "sweepsubject"
""      -&gt; null -&gt; "sweepsubject"      false -&gt; null -&gt; "sweepsubject"
null    -&gt; null -&gt; "sweepsubject"      NaN   -&gt; null -&gt; "sweepsubject"
real handle "sweep-task-r1" -&gt; "sweep-task-r1" -&gt; "sweep-task-r1"
```
There is no falsy string that is a meaningful task-id, so nothing meaningful changed — and `||` is *strictly safer* than `??` for the type-violation cases (`0`/`false`/`NaN`), which `??` would have rendered as literal `"0"`/`"false"`/`"NaN"` labels.

**Observation, deliberately NOT raised as a finding:** a whitespace-only `" "` is truthy and still renders a blank-looking label. I checked reachability before deciding: `Envelope.psm1:138` `.TrimEnd()` + `:154` `IsNullOrEmpty → $null` means an all-whitespace task-id never reaches a record. It is *less* reachable than the `""` case I raised, which was already only a minor. Raising it would be ratcheting, so I am recording it, not charging it.

## FINDINGS

### R2-M1 (MAJOR) — the new README claims an image proves something it does not show

`docs/screenshots/2026-07-26-view-28-12-candidates/README.md:21-23`:
&gt; `health-trend-and-provenance.png` - the #28 ticket-link/**record-dir** provenance anchors and the #12 car health-trend badge, **both rendering** against a controlled review-round fixture.

**I opened the image.** The `#12` ticket link renders as a real anchor (visibly underlined under the train title). The health-trend badges render (`▼ converged (3→0)`, `● stalled (3→4→4)`). But every dispatch-row subject (`conv-review-r1`, `stall-review-…`) and every car chip (`conv-review-r1 Gate returned REJECT round 1`) renders as **plain text with no anchor affordance** — no dotted underline, in direct visual contrast to `#12` in the same frame.

`board/web/verify-registers.mjs:109-114` — the file this same README cites at line 7 — says so explicitly:
&gt; DISCLOSED: this scratch store lives under os.tmpdir(), outside the repo root, so config.githubArtifactsPrefix resolves empty here … **car/gate/dispatch RECORD-DIRECTORY links do NOT render in this particular screenshot for that reason** (proven separately, in-repo, by board/server's TestBuildSnapshotGitHubConfigConfigured and this file's own dom-writer.test.js suite)

So the README **misattributes evidence**: it tells a reader this repo holds a visual capture proving record-dir provenance anchors render, when the capture proves the opposite and the real proof lives in two named tests elsewhere. Under the ruling that landed this morning, a screenshot README's *entire* function is to state truthfully what its images validated — "an image with no record of what it validated … is not evidence at all". A wrong record is worse than none. This is the same overclaiming class as my round-1 Major, one layer over, and it is a defect the round-1 fix created. **Remedy is one clause:** name only the ticket link and the badge, and note that record-dir anchors do not render here (per `verify-registers.mjs:109-114`), with the in-repo proof pointed at instead.

### R2-m1 (minor) — R1-m2 is DRIFTED: the test still cannot fail for a length reason

The fixture and name changed; the substance my finding named did not. Measured **entirely out of repo**, replicating the car's own fixture builder against the real Go server and real Chromium at 1024×900:

```
LANDED (79 chars)    len= 79  scrollWidth= 741  clientWidth=123  -&gt;  scrollWidth&gt;clientWidth = PASS
ROUND-1 (14 chars)   len= 14  scrollWidth= 131  clientWidth=123  -&gt;  scrollWidth&gt;clientWidth = PASS
```

**Direction A holds** (removing `text-overflow: ellipsis` from `board.css` reds the test — injected and observed). **Direction B fails:** shortening the id back to round-1's 14-char `view-75-car-r1` **still passes**. `.solari-subject`'s box is 123px — a 13rem `auto-fill` grid column — so essentially any plausible id overflows it. The new assertion is length-*sensitive* in principle but not length-*discriminating* in the range that matters, which is precisely what my round-1 finding said ("it overflows by 8px, by accident of the 13rem grid column, not because the id is long"). The test name (`browser-dispatch-row-taskid.test.js:79`) still asserts that being "GENUINELY long (79 chars, longer than the hash it replaces)" is what produces the truncation; measured, it is not.

The instrument did improve and now asserts a true, non-vacuous thing — hence minor, not escalated. **Cheapest honest fix (NIRTS):** rename to what it proves — the task-id genuinely overflows its box and the box truncates — rather than engineering a width at which a short id fits.

### R2-m2 (minor) — an unmeasured size claim in a new comment

`board/web/test/support/real-board-server.js:471`: "GENUINELY LONG (79 chars, longer than the **33-char** subject hash it replaces)". Measured: the task_id is **79** chars (true), but the subject it replaces in this fixture is `view-75-with-task-id` = **20** chars. The figure 33 matches nothing in the fixture (real record-dir hashes are 17 chars). The load-bearing half is true; the cited number is not. CLAUDE.md's GUIDE STAR scar names "an unmeasured size claim" as one of the four recorded failures this shop writes down.

### R2-m3 (minor) — an inaccurate mechanism gloss in the new README

`docs/screenshots/2026-07-26-register-check-candidates/README.md:17-18`: "(both lanes carrying that dispatch render red)". I opened `hot-yard.png`: **three** live lanes render red (DISPATCHES / GATES / TRAINS, all "stale, 3060s"). And they do so not because they "carry that dispatch" — the in-flight probe is unassigned yard inventory, present in the dispatches lane's data alone — but because `board/server/poll.go:428` `computeLiveFreshness` is a **single board-global computation** over all records and all dispatches, applied to every live lane. A reader learning the freshness model from this caption would draw a false conclusion about the mechanism.

---

## THE FLAKE RULING: genuine fragility, PRE-EXISTING, out of scope — ticket it

**Not merely a flake.** `board/web/test/support/real-board-server.js:616`:
```js
const resolvedPort = port ?? 4700 + (process.pid % 200);
```
That is a **fixed, PID-derived port**, not port 0. `pid % 200` yields only ~50 distinct values for Windows' multiple-of-4 PIDs, and `node --test` runs each of the **five** `browser-*.test.js` files as a separate concurrent process, each spawning a Go server. The observed `bind: Only one usage of each socket address is normally permitted` is Windows `WSAEADDRINUSE` — the exact signature of a fixed-port collision, not random noise.

**But it is entirely pre-existing and untouched by this train.** `git log -L 616,616` attributes that line to `7fabf8a` ("test(board): drive a real browser for issue #31's cascade defect class (#33)") — the file's creation. Neither round of #75 touched it.

**Incidence, measured:** I ran the full board/web suite **four times** at `5d4baae` — 225/225 each, **zero** bind errors — plus every individual-file run in this review. Consistent with an intermittent modulo collision rather than a systematic break.

**Ruling:** real defect, correctly kept out of this train's scope (rewrite-vs-extend and right-sizing both say so), **not a finding against #75**. It should get its own ticket; the remedy is already proven in-house — bind port 0 and read the bound address back, as #79's probe suite does. `dev`'s friction log (`0964d65`) already records this accurately and honestly says it was deliberately not fixed here; that entry is correct as written.

### The mid-flight amendment and my verification of it

The coordinator's binding amendment (member carries `task_id` always, `subject` added by amendment, never replacing `task_id`) arrived while I was still in the schema-investigation phase. Before implementing I **verified rather than assumed** two things it explicitly asked me to check:

- **Schema required-array conflict:** making `task_id` unconditionally required would have broken the six already-landed `artifacts/train-board-v0/intent-*.json` records (subject-only members, no `task_id`). Ran `scripts/store-checks/StoreIntegrity.Tests.ps1` at each candidate schema shape to confirm. Resolved via `anyOf` (task_id or subject), never backfilling history — confirmed 267/267 store-checks pass unchanged.
- **The board join, empirically, twice:** I built `board/server` from source (`go build ./server`) and ran it against real scratch stores via `STARCAR_STORE_PATH`, exactly the #79 probe's method.
  - First run (old schema, `subject` still required): the record was **quarantined** (`record-quarantined`, "missing property 'subject'").
  - Second run (schema updated to allow task_id-only members): **zero board conditions**, but the trains lane rendered `"cars":[], "declaredNotObserved":[]"` — the member was **invisible**, not `declaredNotObserved` as the amendment hoped.

Root cause: `board/assemble/assemble.go`'s `manifestPayload` function reads `mm["subject"].(string)`, type-asserts absent to `""`, and its own `if subject == "" { continue }` guard drops the member from the parsed `members` slice before `Assemble`'s per-member loop ever runs the `dispatchBySubject` lookup that populates `DeclaredNotObserved`. This is a real, disclosed contradiction between the corrected design and current `board/` code. **I did not touch `board/`** (out of scope, confirmed by diff: only `schema/`, `scripts/New-TrainManifest.ps1`, `scripts/tests/` changed). I filed it as **issue #85** and pinned it as a documented red (`scripts/tests/New-TrainManifest.Tests.ps1`'s last `Context`, which builds the real server and asserts today's true — contradictory — behavior).

### TDD honesty note (disclosed deviation)

Strict red-first was interrupted by the necessary schema-contradiction investigation (I had to write the implementation to discover the `Test-Json`-before-`integrity`-exists bug and the `Join-Path`-on-absolute-path bug). To compensate, I captured a **genuine red** after writing the test file: moved `New-TrainManifest.ps1` aside, ran the suite — **18/21 failed** with `CommandNotFoundException` (script not found), the correct reason — then restored the script. Two real implementation bugs surfaced this way and were fixed:
- `Join-Path` doesn't discard a rooted second argument the way `Path.Combine` does — an absolute `-StoreRoot` (TestDrive) produced `IOException`. Fixed with `[System.IO.Path]::IsPathRooted` guard.
- Schema validation was attempted before `integrity` existed, but the base schema requires it — chicken-and-egg. Fixed by validating a throwaway copy with a placeholder `sha256:` value (shape-only check; pattern satisfied identically by the real hash).

# ADJUDICATION 1 — the car disproved the conductor's premise. **The car is right. Say it plainly.**

I did not take this on report. I built `board/server` from source (`go build -o … ./server`, exit 0), constructed a scratch store with a **four-shape member matrix** in one manifest, and read real HTTP `/api/snapshot`.

Observed, verbatim:

```json
{"id":"train:rev76-matrix","title":"Reviewer matrix probe",
 "cars":[{"subject":"bbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbb","role":"car","state":"returned",...,"taskId":"rev76-B-both"}],
 "declaredNotObserved":["ccccccccccccccccc","ddddddddddddddddd"],
 "tickets":["#76"]}
```

Member A (`task_id: rev76-A-taskid-only`, **no subject**) appears in **neither `cars` nor `declaredNotObserved`**, and **zero board conditions** were raised. Members C and D (subject present, no record) *do* reach `declaredNotObserved`. The task_id-only member is silently gone.

**(a) Root-cause analysis: correct AND complete.** `board/assemble/assemble.go:304` `subject, _ := mm["subject"].(string)` type-asserts an absent key to `""`; `:307` `if subject == "" { continue }` drops the member from the returned slice; `Assemble`'s per-member loop at `:106` iterates only survivors, so the `DeclaredNotObserved` append at `:112` is unreachable for that member. Every hop the car named is exactly right, including that the drop precedes the `dispatchBySubject` lookup at `:110`.

**(b) The pin is HONEST.** `New-TrainManifest.Tests.ps1:308-314` asserts `cars.Count = 0` **and** `declaredNotObserved.Count = 0` — today's real behaviour, not the hoped-for behaviour. That is Law 1 done properly: it gives a future board fix a red to turn green rather than asserting a fiction. I fault-injected it (F1) and it reds correctly when the producer stops emitting subject-less members.

**(c) Staying out of `board/` was the RIGHT call.** `board/` was excluded by the dispatch comment; #79's probe guards that surface; and the fix is a design question about what a subject-less member should render as. Filing #85 (verified: OPEN, `area:view`, on the project board, technically accurate) rather than improvising is the honest-stop doctrine working.

**This is the second time in two days a car has disproved a conductor premise by running the thing. That is the process working, and it is the best part of this diff.** The conductor's binding amendment asserted the behaviour "needs no board change"; the car checked instead of trusting, and the conductor's sentence was wrong.

# ADJUDICATION 2 — the schema contract change. **This is where the diff fails.**

**(b) The `anyOf` DOES enforce what it claims — measured, all seven directions:**

| Member shape | Result |
|---|---|
| `task_id` + `role` | **VALID** |
| `subject` + `role` (legacy) | **VALID** |
| both + `role` | **VALID** |
| **neither**, `role` only | **REJECTED** — `Required properties ["task_id"] are not present` \| `… ["subject"] …` |
| `task_id`, no `role` | REJECTED |
| `subject`, no `role` | REJECTED |
| `{}` | REJECTED |

`role` stays unconditionally required in every combination. The gate is real, not decorative.

**(c) NO-BACKFILL doctrine HONORED — verified, and the car's evidence is stronger than I first credited.** All six `artifacts/train-board-v0/intent-*.json` records validate against **both** schemas at HEAD; every one of their 31 members is `subject+role` with no `task_id`. Crucially, `StoreIntegrity.Tests.ps1:121-136` **does** layer `starcar-manifest.schema.json` over every `^train:` record — so the car's "267/267 unaffected" is genuine NO-BACKFILL evidence, not a base-schema-only result. No landed record was retrofitted or retroactively invalidated. **This part is right.**

### **(a) The relaxation is NOT demonstrably necessary — R1-M1 (Major).**

The brief forbade quietly relaxing `required` to make an implementation pass. The car did not do it *quietly* — it reasoned on the page in three places. But it never examined the alternative that removes the need entirely, so the widening is unjustified rather than merely undocumented.

**The unexamined alternative:** declare a plan member with `task_id` **and** `subject` both set to the shop-minted handle; amendment then overwrites `subject` with the runtime id while `task_id` survives untouched. I built and ran it:

```json
{"id":"train:alt-shape","title":"Alternative shape","cars":[],
 "declaredNotObserved":["tooling-76-car-r1","tooling-76-review-r1"],"tickets":["#76"]}
```

Measured properties of the alternative:
- **Validates against the PRE-#76 `required: ["subject","role"]` schema — `True`.** Zero contract widening; `task_id` lands as a purely additive optional property.
- Renders **readable human names** in `declaredNotObserved` — precisely the surface the corrected design demanded ("*renders in declaredNotObserved with a READABLE name … that IS the plan-vs-actual surface*").
- Zero board conditions, zero board changes.
- Satisfies the mid-flight amendment's actual *requirement* — "a member carries BOTH identities … the human name is stable before launch, in flight, and after return" — because `task_id` is never destroyed.

The shipped shape widens the contract to enable a member that is **invisible**; the un-widened alternative delivers the design's stated goal. The car's own rule "*Never a placeholder: a member's subject is either the genuine dispatch id or not present at all*" (`schema/starcar-manifest.schema.json`, `members.subject` description) is self-authored in this commit and is what forecloses the alternative — it is not inherited doctrine. The conductor's amendment did invite "*a defensible alternative*" and an honest stop. Having discovered that its chosen shape defeats the brief's own purpose, the car had the evidence in hand to escalate; it shipped and ticketed instead.

**(d) Description texts — mostly TRUE, one overclaim (R1-m3).** The `subject` description's board-side disclosure is verbatim accurate against `assemble.go:304-307`; I confirmed every clause. But the `task_id` description asserts "*present on every member declared by New-TrainManifest.ps1 going forward*", and `-Members`' param doc (`:83-85`) calls `task_id` "mandatory". **Measured: a subject-only member mints successfully** (`train:subjonly` written, valid, sealed). Two documents claim an invariant the code does not enforce.

---

# ADJUDICATION 3 — the TDD deviation. **Compensation was weaker than claimed; the tests are nonetheless non-vacuous.**

The car's `CommandNotFoundException` demonstration proves only that the tests *invoke* the script. It does **not** prove behaviour-sensitivity. So I injected six **behavioural** faults on an out-of-repo clone and ran the suite each time (baseline in clone: **21/21 green**).

| Fault (behaviour, not existence) | Result | Red for its own stated reason? |
|---|---|---|
| **F1** mint emits a placeholder `subject` | 18 P / **3 F** | Yes — "*Expected $null or empty, but got string REDACTED*"; also reds the board-gap pin |
| **F2** seal over NON-compact JSON (2nd hashing rule) | 20 P / **1 F** | Yes — integrity mismatch, `sha256:38b38a…` vs `sha256:ac31bb…` |
| **F3** amend replaces `task_id` instead of adding `subject` | 20 P / **1 F** | Yes — the task_id-keyed lookup finds nothing |
| **F4** amend writes in place (no supersession) | 13 P / **8 F** | Yes — byte-identical, new-record, fold-winner and non-vacuity tests all red |
| **F5** schema validation skipped | 18 P / **3 F** | Yes — both `anyOf`/`role` rejection tests red |
| **F6** amend silently no-ops on unmatched task_id | 20 P / **1 F** | Yes — the named-error test reds |

**Ruling: every test I probed is behaviour-sensitive; none passes regardless of behaviour.** The suite is genuinely non-vacuous, so the deviation caused no coverage harm — but the car's own compensation did not establish this, and the report presents it as if it did. Disclosed-and-weaker, not disclosed-and-wrong. I record it as a Note, not a finding, because the substance holds.

**Restore verified byte-identical:** clone `git status` = **0 dirty**; restored `scripts/New-TrainManifest.ps1` sha256 = `9BFC4097E2363D8B3796B7C3B1471CA806F840579893EE607DD2C1D9E21D5417`. All injection ran on the out-of-repo clone; the review worktree was never mutated.

# ADJUDICATION 4 — the two self-found bugs. **Both fixes are sound; the placeholder leaves no real gap.**

**(i) `Join-Path` rooted-second-argument.** Fix at `:303-307` uses `[System.IO.Path]::IsPathRooted` before combining. Correct, and load-bearing — every absolute `-StoreRoot` in my probes worked.

**(ii) The placeholder-sealed validation copy (`:212-215`).** I tested the gap directly rather than reasoning about it.

- **Definitive test:** minted two records into the clone's **real** `artifacts/` store, then ran the real suite — **store-checks 269/269, 0 failed**. The minted records pass the base schema, the layered manifest schema, **and** the independent integrity recompute in `StoreIntegrity.Tests.ps1:64-70`.
- **Malformed is structurally unreachable:** `Get-Sha256Hex` (`scripts/Artifact.psm1:140`) returns `BitConverter.ToString(...) -replace '-','' | ToLower()` — always 64 lowercase hex, always matching `^sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}$`.
- **Wrong-value hunt:** five hostile titles (non-ASCII `ñ`/`é`, `&lt;b&gt;&amp;&lt;/b&gt;`, embedded quotes + backslashes, an embedded newline, forward slashes) — **all five: `integrityVerifies=True, baseSchemaValid=True, integrityFormat=True`.** No serialization round-trip divergence found.

**Ruling: no practical validation gap.** The placeholder is honest engineering — it validates shape when shape is all that can be validated, and the value it stands in for is generated by a function that cannot produce a non-conforming result.

# THE SENTENCE CHECK — producer → store → scan → fold → assemble → wire → client

| Hop | Location | What crosses |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Member normalise | `scripts/New-TrainManifest.ps1:137-146` | ordered dict, only present keys, fixed order `task_id, subject, role, gate` |
| 2. Payload assembly | `:184`, `:195-199` | `manifest{title,tickets,members}` |
| 3. Pre-write validation | `:212-215` | throwaway copy + placeholder integrity, **both** schemas (`:156-159`) |
| 4. **Seal** | `:217-218` → `scripts/Artifact.psm1:124-141` | `sha256:` + `Get-Sha256Hex(compact body)` |
| 5. Write | `:266-269`, `:271-273` | `&lt;store&gt;/&lt;subject ':'→'-'&gt;/intent-&lt;compactAt&gt;.json`, UTF-8 no BOM |
| 6. Scan | `board/store` recursive `*.json` (per `assemble.go:326-328`) | raw record |
| 7. Fold | `board/fold/algorithm.go:130` | requires record-level `subject`/`kind`; latest-`at` intent wins |
| 8. **Assemble** | `board/assemble/assemble.go:94` → `:286-313` | `manifestPayload` reads **only** `subject`(`:304`), `role`(`:305`), `gate`(`:306`) |
| 9. Wire | `board/assemble/types.go:45` | `DeclaredNotObserved []string \`json:"declaredNotObserved"\`` |
| 10. Client | `/api/snapshot` → `lanes[trains].data.trains[]` | observed live |

### **Where it BREAKS**

**Break 1 (adjudication 1, ticketed as #85):** at hop 8, `assemble.go:307` — a `task_id`-only member is dropped before hop 9 exists for it. `declaredNotObserved` is `[]`, `cars` is `[]`, conditions are `[]`. Confirmed live.

**Break 2 — NOT disclosed anywhere, and it is R1-M2 (Major).** `manifestPayload` (`:304-306`) **never reads `task_id` at all.** The board's only task-id source is `taskIDsBySubject` (`:426-437`) → `taskIDForReturnedSubject` (`:450-462`), which reads `r.Fields["task_id"]` off **returned records exclusively**. Therefore a manifest member's `task_id` — the entire field this commit adds to the contract — **is read by nothing in this repo.**

Measured, an amended member (`task_id: tooling-76-car-r1`, `subject: ad9b6aad6153127f4`) with a dispatched-only record:

```json
"cars":[{"subject":"ad9b6aad6153127f4","role":"car","state":"overdue",
         "at":"2026-07-27T10:05:00Z","recordDir":"ad9b6aad6153127f4"}]
```

**No `taskId` field at all** — in either the trains lane or the dispatches lane. That is precisely the symptom the owner reported on the live board and that triggered the mid-flight redesign. The conductor's amendment asserted "*Naming an in-flight dispatch from its MANIFEST MEMBERSHIP is the non-brittle route, and it is why the both-identities shape matters*". **That sentence is false against the code at HEAD** (`assemble.go:414` even says so: "*dispatched records carry no task_id yet, pending #76's producer work*"). The car verified one clause of that comment by running the board and left the adjacent clause — the one carrying the train's headline goal — unchecked and undisclosed. #85 covers only the `declaredNotObserved` drop.

### The seal — Law 6 CLEAN

`New-TrainManifest.ps1:217-218` is byte-for-byte the idiom at `Produce-Artifact.ps1:364-365`, both calling the single owner `Get-Sha256Hex` (`Artifact.psm1:124-141`, whose own docstring names itself "THE ONE OWNER"). **No second hashing rule introduced.** Independently verified: F2 proves the test detects a changed rule; store-checks 269/269 proves minted records verify under the repo's own independent recompute.

# FINDINGS

### Major (any Major = REJECT)

**R1-M1 — the `required` relaxation is not demonstrably necessary; a measured alternative serves the design without it.**
`schema/starcar-manifest.schema.json:45-49`. Evidence above: the seed-subject-with-task-id shape validates against the pre-#76 `required: ["subject","role"]` (`True`), needs no board change, and renders `declaredNotObserved:["tooling-76-car-r1","tooling-76-review-r1"]` — the readable plan-vs-actual surface the corrected design asked for — where the shipped shape renders the member invisible. The brief asked exactly this question; the answer is that the contract was widened to enable the worse of two shapes.

**R1-M2 — undisclosed: a manifest member's `task_id` is read by nothing, and in-flight naming remains unfixed and untracked.**
`board/assemble/assemble.go:304-306` (task_id absent from `manifestPayload`), `:426-462` (task-id sourced only from returned records). Measured: an in-flight member renders with no `taskId`. The mid-flight comment's manifest-membership-naming premise is unmet; #85 does not cover it; no ticket exists. The car's disclosure names one consequence of "assemble keys strictly on subject" and omits the second, which is the one the owner actually asked to see.

**R1-M3 — stale document: the spec that OWNS this contract was left contradicting it.**
`docs/specs/2026-07-23-yard-board-spec.md:46` still reads `members[]{subject, role, gate?}` — `subject` presented as unconditional (the file's own `?` convention marks optional), and `task_id` absent entirely — under the heading **"## 2. The manifest contract"**, "per `schema/starcar-manifest.schema.json`". The schema's own description simultaneously cites "*Spec 2026-07-23-yard-board S2*" as this contract's origin, so the two documents now cite each other and disagree.
Not a fossil: `docs/doc-map.md:53` classifies `docs/specs/*` as **"LIVING while binding; amendment blocks, never rewrites"**; the spec's header reads `Status: Open`, `rev 2 APPROVED`; and the amendment mechanism is already in the file at **§7b "Plan-rung amendments (binding; supersede the named text above)"** with two amendments recorded, one explicitly reversing prior text. `StoreIntegrity.Tests.ps1:109` cites "spec S7b amendment 2" by name, proving the mechanism is live and consumed. The same-commit rule (NORTH STAR: "the commit that invalidates a document updates that document, in the same commit") applies and was not honored.

### Minor

**R1-m1 — the commit failure path reports a confidently wrong cause.** `scripts/New-TrainManifest.ps1:285-291` pipes git's stdout+stderr to `Out-Null`, then throws `"could not commit … after 3 attempts (index contention?)"` for *any* failure. Observed: an author-identity failure (`fatal: unable to auto-detect email address`) surfaced as the index-contention message. Aggravating: the record is already written (`:273`) and `git add` already succeeded, so the operator is left with an uncommitted-but-staged record and a misleading diagnosis. Law 1 / "error messages must be human-readable and actionable".

**R1-m2 — the "mirrors" claim is inaccurate; PowerShell `ValidatePattern` is case-insensitive.** `:70-74` says the literal "mirrors" the schema pattern; `New-TrainManifest.Tests.ps1:102` attributes enforcement to "an early ValidatePattern gate" and `:108` names the uppercase test accordingly. **Measured: `ValidatePattern('^train:[a-z0-9][a-z0-9-]*$')` ACCEPTS `train:Tooling-76`.** The uppercase rejection comes solely from the schema (proven by F5: with schema validation removed, `train-Tooling-76/` was written to disk). The behaviour is correct; the attribution is wrong in two files.

**R1-m3 — doc claims an invariant the code does not enforce.** `schema/starcar-manifest.schema.json` `members.task_id` description ("*present on every member declared by New-TrainManifest.ps1 going forward*") and `scripts/New-TrainManifest.ps1:83-85` ("*task_id (mandatory)*"). Measured: a subject-only member mints successfully.

**R1-m4 — a new silent-loss path is created and recorded on no gated-surface document.** Before this commit the schema rejected subject-less members, making `assemble.go:307` unreachable for schema-valid records. After it, the schema *admits* a shape the board discards with **zero board conditions**. `docs/contracts/gating-matrix.md` — whose doc-map row reads "*Reviewers reject diffs that leave it stale*" — has no row naming this. Law 4 (silent loss). Partly mitigated by #85.

**R1-m5 — the in-code disclosure is stale on arrival.** `scripts/New-TrainManifest.ps1:40-42` says the gap is "*reported for the owner/conductor to judge and, if warranted, file as a follow-on board-side ticket*"; #85 **was** filed. Neither the script header, the test header (`:17-23`), nor the schema description cites #85, so a reader chasing the disclosure has no pointer to the tracked owner.

## §0 answer, first, with its reasoning

**BOTH — and the format half is NOT written here.** The behavioural half (who writes the plan-to-actual binding, when it can exist at all, which component derives it, what each surface renders when it does not exist) is prose in this document. The format half (identity key, `required` set, join rule, precedence, ambiguity, dedup) is specified as an executable deliverable — a schema delta plus 11 named conformance vectors plus red-first tests — and deliberately not held in prose.

The split is a **constraint**, not my preference. A landed verdict in this repo already ruled on this exact class: *"identity, join keys, dedup and ambiguity are format/protocol — prose cannot hold them"* (`artifacts/reviews/2026-07-24-dual-runtime-design-review-round3-REJECT.md:77`). I invite the reviewer to attack my own §0 as §10 Q6, with the test stated: any join/precedence/ordering/dedup rule appearing as prose outside §5.6 is a §0 violation by me.

## Probes run — every behavioural claim was RUN, not read

All six land in the document at §2b with a re-derivation recipe, because an unlanded probe is vigilance-tier.

- **PR-1 (load-bearing, new).** Shape B against a **real `runtime-id` dispatch** rather than an empty store. Observed: `"cars":[]`, `"declaredNotObserved":["d87-car-r1","d87-review-r1"]` while the same wire carried `{"assigned":false,"outcome":"done","state":"returned","subject":"aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa","taskId":"d87-car-r1"}` — **zero conditions**. The board asserts a member has not departed while holding its returned record. The round-1 reviewer measured shape B only in the epoch where it cannot be wrong.
- **PR-2.** Same store, same run: a `minted-id`-family train renders a full consist, filled gates lane, `assigned:true`, zero conditions — **with no change to anything.**
- **PR-3.** Two subjects returning with one `task_id`: both render, **zero conditions**. No ambiguity guard exists.
- **PR-4.** Legacy corpus: 6 manifests, 10 member subjects, all runtime ids, **0 of their 16 returned records carry `task_id`**; a legacy-shaped manifest binds and renders today.
- **PR-5.** 97 live launch payloads: `tool_input` key set is exactly `description, model, prompt, subagent_type`, `description` populated **97/97**, carrying prose not the handle.
- **PR-6.** Real dispatched record carries no `task_id`; its returned sibling does.

## Premises attacked, and what survived

| Premise | Verdict |
|---|---|
| P1 one field, two masters (the conductor's) | **FALSE, measured.** The single-field pressure comes from the *consumer* — `assemble.go:304-307` reads one key — promoted to a law of the format. |
| P2 join must be string equality on a single field | **Half survives.** Equality yes; on the identity field, no. |
| P3 identity must be a string | **Survives, deliberately.** The structured alternative re-imports mutation. |
| P4 amendment must mutate identity | **FALSE, stronger:** no identity amendment exists to perform. |
| P5 the manifest must name the runtime id | **FALSE. Dissolves the most.** |
| **P6 (mine, nobody had written it)** the binding must be *written* at launch | **False for returned, true for in-flight.** Shape A vs shape B is a disagreement about one missing wire, and neither said so. |
| **P7 (mine)** the conductor will reliably amend | Both rejected shapes are correct only if this holds; neither states it. Vigilance-tier. |
| **P8 (mine)** one problem, one shape | **FALSE, measured (PR-2).** Solved non-problem for the `minted-id` family. |

# ADJUDICATION 1 — the §0 split. **The ruling binds, the citation is true, §5.5 is legitimate — and §5.3 violates the author's own test.**

**The cited verdict is real and it binds.** `artifacts/reviews/2026-07-24-dual-runtime-design-review-round3-REJECT.md:77` is the convergence ruling, and it reads verbatim *"identity, join keys, dedup and ambiguity are format/protocol — prose cannot hold them."* (The design renders the em dash as a hyphen — house style per `CLAUDE.md`, not a misquote.) It binds squarely: it was issued about a dispatched↔returned identity/join model, which is this document's subject. `docs/design/2026-07-24-family-agnostic-harness-design.md:10-20` is indeed the document that obeyed it, and this §0 copies its shape faithfully.

**Applying the author's invited test** (design lines 42-45: *"If the reviewer finds a join, precedence, ordering or dedup rule stated as prose outside §5.6, that is a §0 violation"*), I walked every §5 sentence:

| §5 sentence | Class | Vector pins it? | Ruling |
|---|---|---|---|
| §5.5 the one-sentence join + `task_id`-then-`subject` precedence | join + precedence | **V5, V6, V7** | **LEGITIMATE.** Disclosed as a pointer, pinned three ways. This is what the behavioural half pointing at the executable half is supposed to look like. **Q6 ruled in the author's favour on §5.5 — do not delete it.** |
| §5.5 legacy-clause framing | join | V6 | fine — restatement |
| §6 "the precedence clause would prefer `task_id`" | precedence | V7 | fine — applying a pinned rule, not stating a new one |
| D9 "never picks a winner by recency, order, or precedence" | ambiguity | V8 | fine — a §4 decision, pinned |
| **§5.3 "Every declared member renders, in declared order"** | **ORDERING** | **NOTHING** | **VIOLATION → DR1-M3** |

Ordering is one of the four words §0 itself names as format. §5.6's V1-V11 contain no ordering vector and no vector pinning consist membership at all. So the requirement `#85` becomes is prose-only and unfalsifiable by the executable half.

**The converse, ruled explicitly: NOTHING behavioural has escaped into the format half.** V8/V9/V10 look behavioural (they assert what the board *does*), but each is also stated as a §4 decision in the prose half (D9/D7/D6) and reviewable there. That is the correct arrangement — decision in prose, pin executable — not an escape. One gap in the other direction: §0 defers six items and §5.6 pins five (DR1-m4).
