# ADJUDICATION 2 — PR-1, the load-bearing probe. **CONFIRMED, independently, and the design if anything UNDERSTATES it.**

I did not take this on report. I built `board/server` from source (`go build`, exit 0, go1.26.5), constructed my own store from scratch (16 sealed records, `Get-Sha256Hex` over the compact body via `scripts/Artifact.psm1` — one hashing owner, no second rule), and read real HTTP `/api/snapshot`.

**Observed, verbatim from the trains lane:**

```json
{"id":"train:shapeb","title":"Shape B vs a real runtime-id dispatch","cars":[],
 "declaredNotObserved":["d87-car-r1","d87-review-r1"],"tickets":["#87"]}
```

**Simultaneously, same snapshot, dispatches lane:**

```json
{"assigned":false,"at":"2026-07-27T16:21:31Z","outcome":"done","state":"returned",
 "subject":"aaaaaaaaaaaaaaaaa","taskId":"d87-car-r1"}
{"assigned":false,"at":"2026-07-27T16:27:31Z","state":"dispatched","subject":"bbbbbbbbbbbbbbbbb"}
```

**`board: []` — ZERO board conditions.** Confirmed by direct inspection of the snapshot's `board` key.

**The design understates it.** It says the board "asserts a member has not departed." What a human actually reads is stronger. `board/web/js/dom-writer.js:481` renders that array as the literal sentence **`declared, not yet observed: d87-car-r1, d87-review-r1`** — while the same wire carries `d87-car-r1`'s *returned* record. Not-yet-observed is a claim the board cannot make about a handle whose return it is holding. `schema/yard-snapshot.schema.json:143` describes the field as "Manifest members with no matching store records" — also false here. `constitution.md:14-18` is exact: *"a confident falsehood on a status surface is the worst defect this project can ship."*

**The design's central argument stands, and it inverts the owner's understanding of the two rejected shapes.** The round-1 verdict's R1-M1 measured shape B against a store whose only records were the manifest itself — `artifacts/reviews/2026-07-27-tooling-76-review-r1-REJECT.md:134-142`, where the alternative-shape probe output is `"cars":[]` with no runtime-id records present at all. That is the epoch where shape B cannot be wrong. Put in front of a real `runtime-id` dispatch it produces a Law-1 falsehood, which is a **worse class** than shape A's Law-4 silent drop: a silent drop says nothing; shape B says something false. **The owner nearly adopted shape B. It should not be adopted.** State that plainly.

**Non-vacuity of my own reproduction:** PR-1 and PR-2 ran in ONE store, ONE server process, ONE snapshot. The minted-id train rendered perfectly in the same breath the shape-B train rendered falsely — so "the probe store was broken" is ruled out. The author's non-vacuity argument is sound and I reproduced its structure independently.

**PR-2, PR-3, PR-4a — all confirmed in the same snapshot:**

```json
{"id":"train:minted","title":"Minted-id family",
 "cars":[{"subject":"d87-minted-car-r1","role":"car","state":"dispatched",...},
         {"subject":"d87-minted-gate-r1","role":"gate","gate":"car-review r1","state":"returned",
          "outcome":"APPROVE",...,"taskId":"d87-minted-gate-r1"}],
 "declaredNotObserved":[],"tickets":["#87"]}
```

gates lane filled (`{"name":"car-review r1","outcome":"APPROVE"}`), both minted rows `"assigned":true`, zero conditions, zero changes to anything. **P8 holds: the identity crisis is one family's property, not the manifest's.**

PR-3: `ccccccccccccccccc` and `ddddddddddddddddd` both returned with `"taskId":"dup-handle-r1"`, **both render, zero conditions.** No ambiguity guard exists today; D9/V8 is required, not optional.

PR-4a: `train:legacy` — both runtime-id members bound and rendered with liveness, `declaredNotObserved:[]`, zero conditions. **The fallback clause protects RENDERING, not merely validation.** Confirmed.

# ADJUDICATION 3 — PR-5 and the reopened path. **The path IS reopened. The evidence published for it is FALSE.**

I read the same file the author read: `C:\Users\Chris\git\starcar\.claude\probe-logs\post-task.jsonl`, now 98 lines. Line 97 is the author's own dispatch (`Design #87 member identity`, `_probe_logged_at` 2026-07-27 11:58:10); line 98 is mine (12:25:12). So the author saw lines 1-97.

**Measured `tool_input` key sets — TWO, not one:**

```
[description,model,prompt,subagent_type]              x78
[agent_type,description,mode,model,name,prompt]       x20
```

The 20 are the Copilot compat family, `_probe_logged_at` **2026-07-24 07:18:40 through 2026-07-25 19:08:35** — every one of them logged before the author's own dispatch line. **The design's "every one's `tool_input` key set is exactly `description, model, prompt, subagent_type`" is false against the file it cites, by 20 of 97.** → DR1-M1.

**What survives, and it matters:** `description` populated on **98/98**, min length 17, max 43, **98/98 distinct**, present on *both* families. So D8's actual premise — a dedicated, always-present, conductor-authored key — is TRUE and **stronger than the author claimed**.

**Ruling on the reasoning: the design is RIGHT, and the conductor's statement to the owner was wrong.** `scripts/Produce-Artifact.ps1:278-282` (verified verbatim) forbids *"inventing one (e.g. parsing the free-text prompt)"* — the target is invention-by-parsing where no field exists. Reading a whole dedicated field is a different mechanism. Issue #76's comment told the owner *"the launch payload carries no structured field to read it from"*; measured, it carries `description` on 98/98 and, on the Copilot family, `name` as well. **That claim was false and the design is correct to reopen it.** The design also routes the contract question to the owner as Q2 *without pre-answering it* — the exact discipline the #33 scar exists to enforce, done right this time.

**But `description` is not a clean identity carrier, and the author's own log proves the hazard.** Lines 46 and 47:

```
line 46  name='probe-dup-name'  desc='Probe: duplicate dispatch name A'
line 47  name='probe-dup-name'  desc='Probe: duplicate dispatch name B'
```

That is the round-3 verdict's DR-6 defect measured live in this shop: an operator-chosen launch label reused across two dispatches. `description` is exactly as operator-chosen as `name` was; its 98/98 uniqueness is an accident of prose variety, not a property. There is no schema, no pattern, no validation on it anywhere. §5.7's checkable-mirror argument covers conductor-vs-agent *disagreement on one dispatch*; it does not cover *two dispatches carrying one conductor-written handle*, which lands in D9/V8 — and the design never connects D8 to D9. → DR1-m5.

**One compounding fact the design does not reconcile:** `schema/vectors/adapter/copilot-launch-minted-from-name.json:14` declares `"tool_input": { "name": "47-car-r1", "agent_type": "car", "prompt": "x" }` — **no `description`** — while the live Copilot payloads all carry it. The landed vector and the live capture disagree, and D8 built against that vector would find no `description` on the Copilot path.

# ADJUDICATION 4 — the derived binding. **The mechanism is sound; the lifecycle enumeration is incomplete.**

D5 is the strongest thing in this document. Because the binding is *derived at assemble time* rather than *written at some moment*, it is order-free: a returned record landing before its manifest is minted still binds. That dissolves a class of race the two rejected shapes both carried. Credit where it is due.

Lifecycle events I enumerated and checked against the document:

| Event | Covered? | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Two members share a handle in one manifest | **Yes**, deferred + Q5 | §6 row + §10 Q5; correctly notes JSON Schema cannot express uniqueness over a computed key |
| Dispatch returns with a handle no manifest declares | **Yes** | D7, §6 row, V9 |
| One handle → two records (crash re-dispatch) | **Yes** | D9, V8, PR-3 measured |
| One record → two members / two manifests | **Yes, and partly already built** | `assemble.go:169-187` already raises `manifest-membership-collision`; D9 generalizes it |
| Handle reused across trains | **Yes** — same as above | |
| Fix-cycle rounds | **Yes** — D1 mints a new task-id per round (`-r1`, `-r2`) | |
| Manifest superseded, consist GROWS | **Yes** | P4, the six landed records (1→2→4→6→8→10, verified) |
| Manifest superseded, consist SHRINKS (member removed mid-flight) | **NOT ENUMERATED** | P4 names only growth; §6 has no row. Behaviour is inherited-unchanged (removed member's record flips to unattributed → D7 discloses it), so honest — but undesigned |
| **`presumed-lost`** | **NOT ENUMERATED ANYWHERE** | see below |

**`presumed-lost` is the gap worth naming.** It is a first-class kind (`schema/vectors/valid-presumed-lost.json`, `Detect-Dispatches.ps1:163-164` precedence `returned &gt; presumed-lost &gt; dispatched`), it is the record for the #32 killed-session class the owner's ruling says the manifest exists to make detectable, and **it carries no `task_id`** — `Produce-Artifact.ps1:358` writes `task_id` only when `$Kind -eq 'returned'`, and the canonical vector has none. Under D5 a presumed-lost record therefore falls back to `subject` (a runtime id) and binds to no member. §5.1's table lists it in the dispatch stream and §5.2's three epochs, §6's failure table, and §5.6's V-set never mention it again. Under D6/D7 the board would still render two true statements, so it is not a Law-1 hole — but the design's own headline use case (the killed session) routes through a record kind the document never traces. It belongs in §5.2 and §6 explicitly, and D8's stamping question ("does a presumed-lost record inherit its dispatched sibling's `task_id`?") is unasked.

Corpus check, read-only over the real store: **0 presumed-lost records exist today**, so nothing is broken now — this is a design-completeness finding, not a live defect. Folded into DR1-m4's class as a note rather than its own Major, because the rendering stays honest.

---

# ADJUDICATION 5 — the legacy clause and NO-BACKFILL. **Every number verified. The clause is necessary and it protects rendering.**

Measured over the real `artifacts/` store, read-only:

| PR-4 claim | Observed |
|---|---|
| 6 manifests | **6** (`intent-20260723T134605Z` … `T190156Z`) |
| member counts 1, 2, 4, 6, 8, 10 | **exact** |
| 10 distinct member subjects, all runtime ids | **10**, every one keyed `[subject\|role]`, all `aXXXXXXXXXXXXXXXX` form |
| those subjects own 16 returned records | **16** |
| **0 carry `task_id`** | **0** |

Corpus-wide for context: 177 returned records, 96 carry `task_id`; 83 dispatched records, **0** carry `task_id`.

**Does the fallback protect RENDERING and not merely validation? YES — measured (my PR-4a above).** A legacy-shaped manifest through the real board renders both members bound with liveness, `declaredNotObserved:[]`, zero conditions. Without the fallback clause those two rows would vanish from the consist while their records still sat in the store — Law 4 silent loss on a rendered surface.

**Does anything in this design retroactively invalidate a landed record? NO.** D4 keeps `subject` a legal identity key (`anyOf`), D3 never rewrites a landed member, §7 forbids retro-adding `task_id` outright. The one theoretical unbind path — §6's "a legacy manifest meets a modern record for the same subject carrying a `task_id`" — is **measured unreachable** (0 of 16) and structurally unreachable (runtime ids are not reissued). The author recorded it rather than dismissing it, and V7 pins the precedence so a future change reds. That is the right handling.

**Ruling on Q4 (is the fallback worth its price?): YES, and the author's reading of NO-BACKFILL is correct, not a stretch.** Law 4 is explicit — *"a missing lane reading as 'no trains' is a lie of omission"* (`constitution.md:33-37`). A rendered train silently losing its consist is the same class one level down. The author flagged this reading as "mine, not the doctrine's literal text, and it deserves attack"; attacked, it holds.

---

# FINDINGS

## Major (any Major = REJECT)

### **DR1-M1 — PR-5's key-set measurement is false, and §2c's Copilot row is falsified by the very file PR-5 read.**

Design lines **239** (§2b PR-5), **402-406** (§5.7), **65** (§1 C3R-1e row), **260** (§2c row 2).

Claim: *"97 payloads captured; every one's `tool_input` key set is exactly `description, model, prompt, subagent_type`."* Measured over the same file (98 lines; the author saw 1-97): **two** key sets, `description,model,prompt,subagent_type` ×78 and `agent_type,description,mode,model,name,prompt` ×20, the latter logged 2026-07-24 07:18:40 → 2026-07-25 19:08:35, all before the author's own dispatch at 11:58:10 on 07-27.

Three consequences, in ascending order of cost:

1. **A confident falsehood published three times** in a document whose §2b explicitly promises *"every behavioural claim in this document is traceable to an observation rather than a reading."* Law 1; documentation ranks equal to code.
2. **§2c states "No Copilot dispatch is available to me"** and that the tree holds *"only the fossil quotation"* — while 20 Copilot launch payloads with `tool_input.name` populated (`car46-fix-cycle`, `47-car-r1`, `probe-dup-name`, …) sat in the file PR-5 was reading. A probe row declares unprovable a fact the author's own data proves, and P8's minted-id half is told to rest on a fossil when live evidence exists. A downstream car will spend a dispatch re-deriving this.
3. **The design hazard.** An implementer taking §5.7's "key set is exactly" literally writes a Claude-only adapter in a repo whose #47 design exists to be family-agnostic. Compounding: `schema/vectors/adapter/copilot-launch-minted-from-name.json:14` carries no `description` while the live Copilot payloads all do — the landed vector and the capture disagree and the design reconciles neither.

**What survives:** `description` populated 98/98 across BOTH families. D8's premise is intact and stronger than claimed. **Fix is a correction, not a redesign** — but the correction must reach all four locations and must flip §2c's Copilot row from "unverifiable" to "measured".

### **DR1-M2 — the design's Law-1 requirement is traced to the wire and never to the surface a human reads; §8 names no view file.**

The sentence check, every hop with file:line:

| Hop | Location | What crosses |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `board/assemble/assemble.go:110-113` | unbound member → `train.DeclaredNotObserved` |
| 2 | `board/assemble/types.go:45` | `DeclaredNotObserved []string \`json:"declaredNotObserved"\`` |
| 3 | `schema/yard-snapshot.schema.json:140-144` | *"Manifest members with no matching store records"* |
| 4 | **`board/web/js/dom-writer.js:479-482`** | **renders the literal sentence `declared, not yet observed: &lt;names&gt;`** |
| 5 | `board/web/js/render.js:248` | `isTrainTerminal` → false while the list is non-empty |

**D6 (line 298) requires the rendered state be *"declared; no departure bound"* and forbids anything the board cannot know.** "not yet observed" is precisely such a claim — my PR-1 run has hop 4 rendering it about a handle whose returned record is on hop 2 of the same snapshot. §7 (line 453) scopes out renaming the **wire field** and says "Named so it is not assumed done"; the **view string at dom-writer.js:481 is a different artifact** and appears nowhere in the document. §8 (lines 464-474) lists `board/assemble/assemble.go` and `schema/yard-snapshot.schema.json` but **no `board/web/**` file and no `board/web/test/**`** — 209 node tests with no named owner.

Second, structural half: §5.3 requires every member render **"in the consist"**. `yard-snapshot.schema.json:137` requires `["subject","role","state","at"]` on every `cars` item — an unbound member has neither `state` nor `at`. So D6 cannot be implemented without either relaxing that required set or keeping the two-array split it argues against, and §8's wire row ("Wire shape for the member identity title and the new conditions") names neither.

This is the sentence-check scar's own shape: sixteen correct files and a third hop nobody's review reached. The design's *entire case against shape B* is an argument about a rendered sentence; it must own the last hop.

### **DR1-M3 — §0's own test fires: an ORDERING rule is stated as prose outside §5.6, pinned by no vector.**

Design line **337**: *"Every declared member renders, **in declared order**, titled by its identity."* The author's invited test (lines 42-45) names ordering as one of four triggers, and §0 line 23 lists ordering among what prose cannot hold. §5.6's V1-V11 contain **no ordering vector and no vector pinning consist membership at all** — V10 pins only that an unbound member "renders under its identity", which the current two-array wire already satisfies while destroying declared order (a member goes into `cars` *or* `declaredNotObserved`; the interleaving is unrecoverable).

So the requirement `#85` becomes — the single deliverable this design exists to define — lives only in prose, in the behavioural half, with no executable landing, no §8 owner, and no wire shape that can express it. That is the founding scar's exact mechanism, caught by the author's own instrument, which is the one gate that could catch it.

**Ruled the other way, deliberately:** §5.5's one-sentence join+precedence statement is legitimate and should NOT be deleted. It is disclosed as a pointer and pinned three ways by V5/V6/V7. **Q6 answered: the split is honest everywhere except §5.3.**

## Minor

**DR1-m1 — mis-citation, off by one row.** Line 61 cites *"LIVING per `docs/doc-map.md:52`"* for the SPEC. Line 52 is the `docs/design/*` row (*"LIVING while its train runs; FOSSIL revisions in git history"*); the `docs/specs/*` row is **line 53** (*"LIVING while binding; amendment blocks, never rewrites"*). The design depends on the *spec* axis to make §8's amendment obligation binding, and the round-1 verdict it inherits cited `:53` correctly (`…-tooling-76-review-r1-REJECT.md:232`). Note for the record: `CitationResolverPolicy.Tests.ps1:12-18` declares this class explicitly outside its binding checks — *"an in-range shifted citation … is not detected by any BINDING check here"* — so 43/0/4 green does not clear it, and the gate says so itself.

**DR1-m2 — §2c parks two desk-provable facts in the probe list.** Row *"Handle collisions … Requires a corpus that does not exist yet"*: measured by me in one command over `artifacts/` — **96 records carry `task_id`, 96 distinct values, ZERO shared by two subjects.** The corpus exists and is 96 handles deep; nothing about counting it required §5.7 to land. Row *"No two handles differ only by case or whitespace — I did not enumerate"* (and §10 Q3): measured — **0 uppercase, 0 whitespace, 0 case-collisions across all 96.** **Q3 is settled: exact string equality is sufficient today; no canonicalisation vector is needed.** The template's rule is that *unverifiable* claims go in §2c; parking verifiable ones there inflates the probe list and buys downstream dispatches. (This does not weaken D9/V8 — the design correctly declines to assume rarity, and that stance is right.)

**DR1-m3 — §5.4's "Unchanged for bound records" is imprecise about `assigned`, the symptom that started this train.** `assemble.go:108` sets `assignedSubjects[m.Subject]` from the MEMBER's identity. Under D5 it must key off the BOUND record's subject instead, so `assigned` flips false→true for every runtime-id member that binds. Measured in my PR-1 snapshot: the shape-B runtime-id records render `"assigned":false` while the minted-id records render `"assigned":true`. §8's assemble.go row lists "the join, the no-drop rule, the two conditions" and not the assignment rewire — and #76's own probe comment names "92 unassigned" as one of the three symptoms this work exists to fix.

**DR1-m4 — §0 defers six items; §5.6 pins five.** §0 lines 22-24 defer the identity key, the `required` set, the join rule and precedence, ambiguity, duplicates, **and "what 'amendment' may and may not touch."** §5.6's "must pin, at minimum" list has no amendment vector. One deferred obligation lands nowhere — the carrier rule's own failure shape, inside a single document. **Related, and worth folding into the same fix:** `presumed-lost` appears in §5.1's table and then in no epoch (§5.2), no failure row (§6), and no vector (§5.6), despite being the record kind for the killed-session class the owner's ruling names as the manifest's purpose, and despite carrying no `task_id` by construction (`Produce-Artifact.ps1:358`, `schema/vectors/valid-presumed-lost.json`). 0 exist in the store today, so nothing is live-broken.

**DR1-m5 — D8 re-imports P7's vigilance dependency and the design never says so.** P7 (lines 179-186) kills both rejected shapes because *"a mechanism whose correctness depends on a human remembering a step per dispatch is vigilance-tier."* D8 (line 300) is a convention requiring the conductor to write the handle into `description` on every dispatch. D5 genuinely kills vigilance for the returned epoch — verified, the data is already there. D8 restores it for the in-flight epoch, and the document never acknowledges the symmetry. The defence is available and probably right (vigilance-with-honest-degrade under D7 ≠ vigilance-with-silent-falsehood), but it must be *made*, against the author's own premise. Aggravating, from the author's own log: `tool_input.name = 'probe-dup-name'` on two distinct dispatches (lines 46-47) — the round-3 verdict's DR-6 defect, measured live in this shop. `description` is exactly as operator-chosen as `name` was; the design never wires D8's collision risk to D9/V8.

- **Law 1 (`constitution.md:14-18`, quote verified)** — *Honored* in D6/D7/D9's refusal to guess a pairing, in §6's unknown row (the row the template calls most commonly missed), and in §6's decision to record the theoretically-possible legacy unbind rather than dismiss it. *Violated* at **DR1-M1** (a false measurement published three times, in a document that promises every claim traces to an observation) and *left violated in effect* at **DR1-M2** (D6's requirement never reaches `dom-writer.js:481`, where the false sentence is actually rendered).

- **Law 4 (`:33-37`)** — *Honored, and this is the design's strongest leg.* D6 forbids the drop outright; §5.5's fallback clause is measured (PR-4a) to protect rendering and not merely validation; #85's silent loss is closed by construction rather than by a condition bolted on.
- **Law 6 (`:46-51`)** — *Honored.* One join rule, one owner (the assembler), symmetric on both sides. D9 shows the disagreement rather than picking a winner, which is the clause verbatim. §5.7's one unavoidable mirror is made *checkable* rather than merely tolerated, and the author says outright it would not propose it otherwise. My PR-3 confirms the guard is genuinely absent today.
- **Law 7 (`:53-57`)** — *Honored* in D3's refusal to redefine `subject` (a stranger reads it consistently) and in leaving `schema/vocab/roles.json` the vocabulary owner with no new enum. *Weakened* by **DR1-M1**: a stranger reading §5.7 builds a one-family adapter in a repo whose #47 design exists to be family-agnostic.
- **NO-BACKFILL** — **Honored, and verified rather than assumed.** 6 manifests, 10 subjects, 16 returned records, 0 with `task_id`, all counted by me. Nothing here retroactively invalidates a landed record; §7 forbids retro-stamping outright; the one theoretical unbind path is measured unreachable and pinned by V7.
- **Living contracts / same-commit** — *Not triggered.* The diff adds one document and changes no code, so nothing goes stale on landing. §8 correctly assigns the future obligations, including R1-M3's §7b amendment block as same-commit and non-negotiable — except that it omits the view layer (**DR1-M2**).

# REVIEWER SELF-CORRECTION — the timestamp divergence. **The author is right. I was wrong.**

The brief asked me to check which of us was misreading `_probe_logged_at`. I read the raw bytes with no JSON parser:

```
line 42: "_probe_logged_at": "2026-07-24T11:18:40.178633+00:00"
line 61: "_probe_logged_at": "2026-07-25T23:08:35.962940+00:00"
```

The design's rendering is the correct one. **My round-1 verdict published `07:18:40` and `19:08:35` as if they were the field's values; they are local-time renderings.** Root cause, measured:

```
ConvertFrom-Json gives type: System.DateTime      &lt;- not String
ConvertFrom-Json renders as : 07/24/2026 07:18:40
raw substring from the line : "2026-07-24T11:18:40.178633+00:00"
local TZ: Eastern Standard Time (-05:00 base, -04:00 in July)
```

`ConvertFrom-Json` silently coerces an ISO-8601 string to a local `System.DateTime`. I never looked at the raw bytes. Same 20 records, same span — but I stated a coordinate I had not actually observed, in a verdict whose whole standard is that behavioural claims are quoted from observation. **This is the fourth member of the family the hazard list already names: a tool's implicit conversion, invisible until you read raw bytes.** It belongs in `docs/friction-log.md` beside the other three, and I recommend it lands there — it is not the design author's defect and I do not charge it against the document.

The author disclosing a divergence from its reviewer's verdict, rather than quietly adopting my numbers, is the behaviour this shop wants. Recording it as a win for the author and a miss for me.

### 1. DR1-M1 — the per-family rewrite. **The producer claim is TRUE and materially shrinks D8.**

```powershell
} elseif (-not [string]::IsNullOrWhiteSpace($launchAgentType)) {

```

Exactly as claimed. And my own round-1 PR-2 already measured the consequence end to end: a train whose members are shop handles and whose dispatch records' `subject` IS that handle rendered a full consist with `assigned:true` and a live `state:dispatched` member row, zero conditions, no change to anything. **The in-flight epoch is genuinely already closed for the Copilot family.** D8 narrows from "the runtime-id problem" to "the Claude family only". That is a real reduction in scope, correctly derived from the corrected measurement — the finding strengthened the design, as the author says.

**But the narrowing broke three statements elsewhere → DR2-M1.**

### 2. DR1-M2 — **§5.3a genuinely OWNS the rendered string; it does not describe and stop.**

Ownership test, six ways: it names the exact file:line (`dom-writer.js:481`); quotes the literal false string; states the requirement (say only what the board can back); **names the replacement wording** (*"no departure bound"*); assigns an owner; and binds it to the SAME commit as the wire change with the reason stated at §8:711 — *"a wire change without the view leaves the false sentence rendering off a renamed field."* That last clause is the one that proves it is ownership rather than description: it anticipates the failure mode of splitting the work.

**Is the proposed consist shape implementable against the wire it names?** Yes. `yard-snapshot.schema.json:137` requires `["subject","role","state","at"]`; the proposal relaxes `at` to optional and gives `state` an unbound value. The cited precedent is real and I verified it: `recordDir` (`:128-131`, *"Absent when no surviving record names this subject"*) and `taskId` (`:132-135`, *"Absent when this car has not yet returned with one"*) are both optional-when-unknowable and outside the required set. The pattern exists on this exact object.

**Does routing the alternative to Q7 hide a decision? No — the design MAKES the choice** (§5.3:437-441) and routes only the *alternative* for attack, stating "I am genuinely unsure, this is the round-2 decision most likely to be wrong." That is deciding and inviting rejection, which is the opposite of hiding.

**MY Q7 RULING: take the single ordered consist.** The decisive argument is Law 6, which the design gestures at but does not lean on: two arrays that every consumer must re-merge is one structure maintained in two places, and the merge rule gets reimplemented by the view, the node tests, and every future adapter. The `declaredIndex` alternative also leaves the unbound member outside the array a human reads — which is most of what §5.3a exists to fix. The cost (relaxing a required set) is bounded and precedented. **One carry for the implementing car:** `render.js:247-248`'s `isTrainTerminal` reads `declaredNotObserved.length` and must be rewritten against the new shape rather than deleted — §8:711 already flags this, correctly.

### 3. DR1-M3 — **V12 is writable by a stranger, and "red on arrival" is honestly achievable, not aspirational.**

V12 (:557) supplies the fixture concretely — a 4-member manifest declared car/gate/car/gate with members 1 and 3 bound, 2 and 4 unbound — and the assertion: every declared member, exactly once, in declared order, interleaved.

**Red on arrival is achievable.** Today `cars` = [m1, m3] and `declaredNotObserved` = [m2, m4]. Any accessor a vector author picks — the natural one being concatenate-and-compare — yields [m1, m3, m2, m4] ≠ [m1, m2, m3, m4]. The declared interleaving is unrecoverable from the two-array wire, so the vector cannot accidentally pass. That is exactly what the author means by "a V12 that passes before the wire changes has been written to the wire instead of to the requirement."

**Critically, V12 is written to the REQUIREMENT, not to the shape** — which is why it survives Q7 being open. Under either Q7 answer the assertion "declared order recoverable, every member exactly once" is expressible. That is the right level for a design rung. The one thing left to the vector author is how to *read* "the consist" from a given wire; acceptable, and unavoidable while Q7 is unruled.

### 4. The minors.

**m2 — genuinely settled, not declared settled.** All six PR-7 numbers re-derived by me at `3c31ed0` and exact. Q3's closure at :804-814 is the honest form: it states the ruling (exact string equality sufficient), then **bounds it** — *"the corpus is 96 handles deep and days old, and nothing structurally prevents an uppercase handle"* — and names what reopens it. A settled question that carries its own expiry condition is settled properly.

**m4 — the mapping table does NOT prove all six land → DR2-m1.** Five rows are clean. Row 5 ("ambiguity and duplicates") lands the record-side duplicate in V8 but routes the **intra-manifest member duplicate to §10 Q5, an open question, not a vector.** The table discloses this in parentheses; the bolded sentence above it claims all six land in vectors. Overstated by its own table. Also: V12 is filed under "the record-to-member join rule", which it is not — it pins consist order, a rendering concern; the row is carried by V5/V6 alone. Harmless padding, but it inflates the appearance of coverage on the one claim that failed last round.

**m5 — §5.7a is a REAL argument, not a hedge.** Four tests, all passed: (a) it states the symmetry *first* and defends *second*, rather than leading with the defence; (b) the distinction it draws — P7 condemns vigilance whose failure mode is a *falsehood*, not vigilance as such — is substantive, and I verified the D8 row of its table is factually right (prose stamped as `task_id` binds to no member → unattributed → D7 condition → epoch 2; loud and true); (c) it names its own load-bearing dependency out loud (*"it holds only because D7 exists, which is why D7 is not optional garnish"*), which makes it falsifiable; (d) **it quotes evidence against itself** — `probe-dup-name` on two dispatches, from its own capture — and wires D8's collision risk explicitly to D9/V8 while routing prevention-vs-disclosure to Q2. A hedge does not go looking for its own counter-evidence.

### 5. The two self-disclosures.

**(a) The unresolvable verdict path — the disclosure is SUFFICIENT, and it is the correct form.** Verified: `artifacts/reviews/2026-07-27-design-87-review-r1-REJECT.md` is **absent** at `3c31ed0`, **present** at `8c90dbe` (*"harness: land design-87 review r1 verdict (REJECT 3M 5m; law-first diagnostic CLEAN) + index"*) and on `dev` tip. The author verified presence rather than assuming it, and supplied a working `git show dev:...` retrieval.

Ruling: not a defect. The carrier rule *requires* the verdict be named; the alternative — omitting it — is strictly worse. The condition is transient and self-heals at merge, and the disclosure converts an invisible dangling reference into a visible actionable one, which is precisely what Law 1 asks. **No gate catches it because the citation carries no line number and the resolver only checks `file:line` coordinates** — which I confirmed. **One durable observation, not a finding:** this class recurs on *every* delta-reviewed design, since the round-N verdict always lands on `dev` after the branch was cut. The lasting fix is a convention in the design template, not a per-document disclosure. Worth a template note.

**(b) The restore that did not run — verified genuinely restored.** `F7BD34D842D55770DCAE2297F868A7FBF2DFF02D0A04337FD9ED9C2BE97F4436`, matching the author's report, and the working file equals the committed blob (0 dirty). The author caught this by checking the hash rather than trusting the block — which is the whole lesson, and I adopted the same discipline for my own injection above.

# FINDINGS

## Major (any Major = REJECT)

### **DR2-M1 — the round-2 §5.7 rewrite makes the fallback clause load-bearing for a LIVE runtime family, and three other places still call it legacy-only. Q4 now asks the reviewer to rule on a false cost basis.**

The chain, verified end to end:

1. **§5.7:593-596** establishes that Copilot dispatched records carry the shop handle in `subject`, via `Produce-Artifact.ps1:186-190` (`$subject = Get-Prop $toolInput 'name'`, `subject_basis = minted-id`) — verified.
2. **No dispatched record of any family carries `task_id`** — `Produce-Artifact.ps1:358` writes it only when `$Kind -eq 'returned'`; corpus confirms 83 dispatched, 0 with `task_id`.
3. Therefore under **§5.5:505-507**, a Copilot dispatched record resolves `task_id` (absent) → **falls back to `subject`** = the handle, and binds to the member whose `task_id` is that handle. **Every Copilot in-flight binding runs through the fallback clause, permanently.**

Three statements are now false or unreachable:

- **§5.5:507-508** — *"The fallback is what keeps the legacy corpus rendering; it is a legacy clause, not a general escape hatch."* It is now the permanent binding mechanism for a live family.
- **§10 Q4:816-817** — *"It exists **solely** for six landed manifests and 10 dispatch subjects."* False. And Q4 asks me to rule on dropping it, offering the cost as *"the `train:board-v0` records stop rendering their consist"*. **The true cost of dropping it is that the entire minted-id family stops binding in flight.** A reviewer or owner ruling on the stated basis would delete a clause a live family depends on — decision-corrupting, in the section whose job is to surface soft spots accurately.
- **§7:691** — stamping `task_id` on minted-id records is *"hygiene, not correctness"*, triggered by *"the D8 wire landing"*. But §5.7 now says Copilot needs no D8 work at all, so if D8 lands Claude-only the trigger never fires and the fallback is permanent by construction rather than by decision.

**This is a defect the round-2 fix created** — the sharpest of the three swirl conditions. The remedy is cheap (three sentences: recharacterize §5.5, restate Q4's cost, re-trigger §7), which is why this is a fixable REJECT and not an escalation.

*For the record, my Q4 ruling under the corrected basis: the fallback clause is not merely worth its price, it is now structural. Keep it, and stop calling it legacy.*

### **DR2-M2 — V13 (`amendment-scope`) is unsatisfiable as specified: the operation it forbids is observationally identical to one it permits, and no enforcer is named.**

**§5.6:558** — *"A superseding manifest MAY add, remove or reorder members and MAY change a member's `role`/`gate`; it MUST NOT change an existing member's identity … an amendment that rewrites a member's `task_id` is REJECTED."*

Take manifest v1 with members `[{task_id: A, role: car}, {task_id: B, role: gate}]`. Now compare:

- **v2 (forbidden):** rewrite A's identity to C → `[{task_id: C, role: car}, {task_id: B, role: gate}]`
- **v2' (permitted):** remove member A, add member C → `[{task_id: C, role: car}, {task_id: B, role: gate}]`

**Byte-identical records.** No record-shaped conformance vector can distinguish them, because identifying "an existing member" across a supersession requires an identity — the very thing that changed. §5.6:525-526 declares the deliverable is *"conformance vectors in the landed `schema/vectors/` pattern"*, which are record fixtures. V13 cannot be written in that pattern, and V13 names no enforcer.

The author's non-vacuity note at **:566** half-spots this — *"V13 vacuously because no amendment mechanism exists to violate it yet"* — but attributes it to a temporary condition. **The cause is structural and permanent:** V13 stays vacuous after the amendment mechanism lands, because the forbidden state is indistinguishable from a permitted one in the artifact V13 inspects.

**The fix is cheap and I will name it, since two clean options exist.** Either (a) name the **producer tool** as V13's enforcer — `New-TrainManifest.ps1`'s amend path *does* know the operation because the operator requested it, and the #76 round-1 verdict's F3 injection (*"amend replaces `task_id` instead of adding `subject`"* → 1 red) proves it is testable there — and move V13 out of the schema-vector set with that stated; or (b) drop the MUST-NOT clause as structurally unnecessary, since D2 already makes a member's identity immutable by construction and there is nothing left to pin.

**Also created by the round-2 fix** — V13 is new at round 2, introduced specifically to close DR1-m4's sixth obligation, and it does not close it.

## Minor

**DR2-m1 — "all six now land" overstates its own table.** §5.6:528-529 claims in bold that every §0-deferred obligation now lands in a vector; the table's row 5 (:539) lands the intra-manifest duplicate case in *"§10 Q5"*, an open question routed to the reviewer, not a vector. Disclosed in the table, overstated in the claim — the same class as DR1-m4, one notch smaller, on the exact claim that failed last round. Secondary: V12 is filed under *"the record-to-member join rule"* (:537), which it is not; that row is carried by V5/V6 alone and V12's presence inflates apparent coverage.

**DR2-m2 — §8 omits `schema/starcar-artifact.schema.json`, whose `task_id` description D8 falsifies twice.** `:80-82` reads *"Optional, **returned-only**. … the shop-minted dispatch id **echoed back by the report's starcar-artifact envelope** (`task-id:` line)."* D8 makes `task_id` dispatched-also and **conductor**-sourced rather than envelope-echoed. §8 correctly lists the Copilot adapter vector (`:713`) for exactly this reason — a landed artifact D8 would make wrong — so omitting the schema whose description D8 contradicts head-on is an inconsistency inside the table that otherwise does this job well. Same class as R1-M3, which this train already paid a Major for.

**DR2-m3 — D8 specifies no shape check before stamping `description` into `task_id`.** §5.7:600-603. The conductor writes prose there today (PR-5's own samples: `Design #87 member identity`). If the convention is missed, the producer stamps prose into a field the schema calls *"the shop-minted dispatch id"*. The board degrades honestly — §5.7a's table is right that D7 discloses it, and I verified that path — but the **store** accumulates records whose `task_id` is not a task id, and §5.6 pins no vector for the case. §10 Q2 asks the owner about prevention-vs-disclosure for *collisions* but never about *shape*. One clause in Q2 and one vector would close it.

# CONVERGENCE

**Round 1: 3 Major / 5 Minor / 8 Notes. Round 2: 2 Major / 3 Minor / 6 Notes.** Declining on both axes.

I applied the swirl-and-churn trigger honestly, because two of its three conditions are close:

| Condition | Fires? |
|---|---|
| Major counts stop declining | **No** — 3 → 2 |
| Findings cluster in the same section across rounds | **Partially** — §5.6 and §5.7 appear both rounds, but at different layers and on different concerns (a measurement error → a characterization inconsistency; a *missing* vector → a *defective* vector) |
| A round's findings include defects the previous round's fixes created | **YES — both Majors** |

**Ruling: NOT swirl, but I am setting a CAP.** The countervailing evidence is decisive: the mechanism (D1-D9) is unchanged and has now survived two adversarial rounds with independent measurement behind every load-bearing claim; both new Majors are *documentation-consistency and vector-specification* defects, not mechanism defects; and both fixes are a handful of sentences. Introducing 2 defects while closing 8 across a +347-line revision is a converging ratio, not the 7→3→4→5 flat-or-climbing signature the dual-runtime scar names.

**THE CAP, which only the party holding the series can set:** round 3 is the last round at this rung. **If round 3 produces any Major that is again a defect created by round 2's own fixes, the conductor escalates to the owner rather than dispatching round 4.** That would be the third condition firing twice consecutively, which is the pattern the doctrine exists to catch. Note also that round 4 would independently trip the reviewer-rotation trigger.

**LADDER POSITION — what the next rung must be, on approval.** This is a design, so convergence does **not** mean implementation. Per §0's own split, the next artifact is the **EXECUTABLE format half**: the schema delta plus the V1-V14 conformance vectors plus red-first tests, landing in the `schema/vectors/` pattern. Not a prose spec — §0 already ruled prose cannot hold this half. Two gates before any board code is written:

1. **V12 demonstrated RED ON ARRIVAL** against the current two-array wire, with the failure quoted. It is the one vector whose whole value is failing first, and a green V12 means it was written to the wire instead of the requirement.
2. **Q7 ruled** (I have ruled: single ordered consist), because V12's expected output depends on the wire shape.

The board join/render car (#85/#86) follows the vectors, not the reverse. D8 stays gated on owner Q2 throughout and is separable by design — that separability held up under attack this round and is one of the document's better structural choices.

## MAJOR FINDINGS

### R1-M1 — A freight lane that has NEVER run claims "showing last good" after a scan failure (Law 1)

`board/server/poll.go:365-367` sets `s.lastGoodAsOf["freight"] = newLastGood` on **every** successful scan, and `:398-402` sets `s.lastGoodLaneData["freight"] = assembled.Freight` unconditionally — regardless of whether a `ticket-sync` heartbeat exists. `:330-334` then serves that as the `failed` variant's `LastGoodAsOf`.

**Measured, real browser, real Go binary** (probe: empty scratch store → poll → `renameSync` the store away):

```
BEFORE store loss: freightSecondary = "not yet polled"          register-in-progress
AFTER  store loss: freightSecondary = "source failed (...) - showing last good from 2026-07-27T17:10:36Z"
                   freightBody      = "no tickets in the queue"  register-needs-attention
```

One poll earlier the board itself said the adapter had never run. It now asserts it is showing the last good freight queue, and that it was empty. This is precisely the collapse the issue forbids: *"An empty lane that silently means 'we do not know' is the confident-falsehood class this board exists to prevent."*

The reuse is unexamined and the comment above it proves the car knew the distinction mattered — `poll.go:304-314` argues `freightFreshnessVal` is "DELIBERATELY a separate value… never a reuse", while the `lastGood` bookkeeping 50 lines below **is** a reuse of a lane whose definition of "good" the car deliberately made different. For dispatches/gates/trains a successful scan genuinely *is* good data (DR3-5a honest-empty); for freight it explicitly is not.

`TestPollOnceFreightRetainsLastGoodOnScanFailure` (`poll_test.go:203-233`) cannot catch this — its poll-1 store seeds a heartbeat at `:205`, so it only exercises the case where freight really did have good data.

### R1-M2 — Heartbeat absent + tickets present renders "not yet polled" beside the tickets, reachable by a real adapter failure

`computeFreightFreshness` returns `never-polled` at `poll.go:547-549` purely on heartbeat absence, while `assemble.go:235` populates `Freight.Tickets` from any `kind=ticket` records regardless. Nothing reconciles the two.

**I injected a mid-run failure into the real adapter** (`throw` before the 2nd of 2 ticket writes), ran the real `Sync-Freight.ps1`, and rendered the resulting store in a real browser. Verbatim lane text:

```
4 Freight  the inbound ticket queue  Live  not yet polled  #84 first ticket Backlog
```

The secondary line says the adapter has never run; the body shows a ticket only the adapter could have written. No board condition fires. This is a HAVAGLANCE failure — a single glance transmits two contradictory facts.

This is **not hypothetical**: it is the direct consequence of the car's own heartbeat-written-LAST ordering (`Sync-Freight.ps1:184-185`). It also arises whenever the heartbeat is quarantined while tickets survive — I measured that too (malformed `at` → `record-quarantined`, freight reads `"not yet polled"` with ticket #84 rendered).

My full state-space sweep (11 states, real browser, real binary):

| State | secondary | register | body |
|---|---|---|---|
| S1 never run | `not yet polled` | in-progress | no tickets ✅ |
| S2 ran, empty | `fresh` | nominal | no tickets ✅ |
| S3 stale | `stale, 57593:08:45` | needs-attention | 1 row ✅ |
| **S4 tickets, no heartbeat** | **`not yet polled`** | in-progress | **2 rows** ❌ |
| **S5 malformed heartbeat** | **`not yet polled`** | in-progress | **1 row** ❌ (condition raised) |
| S6 old beat / new tickets | `stale, …` | needs-attention | 1 row ✅ |
| S7 new beat / old tickets | `fresh` | nominal | 1 row ✅ |
| S8 future heartbeat | `not yet polled` | in-progress | empty (2 FLAGS raised) ✅ |
| S9 malformed ticket payload | `fresh` | nominal | empty (quarantined) ✅ |
| S10 store unreadable | `source failed …` | needs-attention | no-renderer ✅ |
| S11 two heartbeats | `fresh` | nominal | latest-wins ✅ |

### R1-M3 — Committed screenshots depict the rendering this diff replaces; the obligation is named, MAJOR-tier, and was not performed

`docs/templates/car-brief.md:124-131` is explicit: *"whenever the diff changes what the board RENDERS, open `docs/screenshots/` — not the enumeration the car reports, the directory itself — and rule on whether any committed image depicts the rendering this diff replaces… An affirmative 'documentation is clear' claim that never opened this directory is itself the finding."* `:122-123` sets the tier: *"A stale document or a dead citation is a MAJOR finding."*

I opened the directory and the images:

- `docs/screenshots/2026-07-23-first-light.png` renders `FREIGHT / Dark / no equipment on this lane`. It is a **bare file with no README at all** — nothing states what it validated or at which commit.
- `docs/screenshots/2026-07-26-register-check-candidates/calm-yard.png` renders `4 FREIGHT / the inbound ticket queue / Dark / no equipment on this lane`, under a README whose line 3 reads **`Status: Current`** and which describes it as *"the nominal register across all five lanes"*.
- `docs/screenshots/2026-07-26-view-28-12-candidates/` carries the same ambient-store `calm-yard.png`/`hot-yard.png` pair under its own **`Status: Current`**.

No screenshot README was touched by this commit and the commit message never mentions screenshots. The remedy the template prescribes is not recapture — it is a README stating the divergence. Note the repo has already paid for this twice (`#69/#71` round 2, `#75` round 1), which is why the clause exists.

### R1-M4 — `Sync-Freight.ps1`'s documented failure contract is measurably false (Law 1)

`scripts/Sync-Freight.ps1:35-38`:

&gt; *"a failed run (the gh call throws, a write fails) writes NOTHING - not even a 'we tried and failed' record - and exits nonzero."*

**Measured false.** Ticket files are written one at a time via `[System.IO.File]::WriteAllText` at `:112` inside the loop at `:170-173`, and departed directories are deleted at `:181` — all before the heartbeat at `:185`. My injected mid-run failure left:

```
\_faults.log
\ticket-84\ticket.json      &lt;- written by a run that "writes NOTHING"
EXIT=1
```

This matters beyond the sentence: it is the **load-bearing premise of Adjudication 1**. The failure posture was accepted on the grounds that a failed run leaves the store untouched, so failure shows up purely as staleness. It does not — it leaves partial state plus a stale-or-absent heartbeat, which is R1-M2.

## THE THREE ADJUDICATIONS, RULED

### Adjudication 1 — failure posture: **the DESIGN is sound; the IMPLEMENTATION does not honor it**

"Stale" *is* an honest rendering of "failing for six hours" for this lane. It is literally true (the last success was N hours ago) and asserts nothing about health. The rejected alternative has a genuine flaw the car identified correctly: a "tried and failed" record carries its own `at` and becomes a second freshness axis that can itself go stale — two records answering one question (Law 6). Law 4 is satisfied: the fault reaches `_faults.log` (`:78-88`) and **is read** — `.claude/skills/goodnight/SKILL.md:90` reads `artifacts/_faults.log` at session close. It is not a decorative guard.

**I do not require a failure record.** But the posture was justified by "a failed run writes NOTHING", which is false (R1-M4), and its falsity produces the contradictory render (R1-M2). Fix the premise (write to a temp tree and swap, or have the board detect tickets-without-heartbeat and say so), not the posture.

### Adjudication 2 — record shape: **CORRECT, and I endorse it. Not silent loss.**

The premise in the question — that the store *loses* history — is **false at the repository level**, and I checked rather than assumed. Every overwrite and every deletion is committed (`git commit --only -- &lt;paths&gt;`, `:205-215`); `git log --follow artifacts/ticket-84/ticket.json` retains every state, and the Entire checkpoint branch mirrors it. Git is the append-only substrate; the working tree is a materialized current-state view.

- **Law 4:** the removal is a committed, message-bearing change, not a silent drop. A ticket leaving Backlog/Todo has *moved to In Progress*, where the trains lane shows it — the information relocates, it does not vanish.
- **Law 6:** the reasoning is verified, not just plausible. `assemble_test.go:960` asserts the *real* fold produces zero dispatches/intents for a ticket-only store, and fails loudly if that ever changes. An event-log shape would therefore force `board/assemble` to reimplement latest-wins. Fixed filename is the right call.
- **`StoreIntegrity`:** re-globs each run; removed files are simply not cases. Ticket records validate and their integrity recomputes — the seal reuses `Artifact.psm1`'s `Get-Sha256Hex` (`:103`) over the same `-Compress` canonical body as `Produce-Artifact.ps1:364-365`, and `StoreIntegrity`'s re-derivation is field-order-agnostic.
- **`New-ArtifactIndex`:** generic over `subject/kind/at/outcome`; ticket records index cleanly, removed ones drop out — consistent with its own "records are the source of truth" header.

Only R1-m6 (index cadence) attaches here.

### Adjudication 3 — the pre-landing probe: **CONFIRMED REAL, re-derived end to end**

Built the **base** binary (base `kinds.json` has no `ticket`; base `store.go` has 0 `json:"ticket"` keys), served a `kind:ticket` record:

```
STEP 1  [needs-attention] record-unrecognised-fields :: ticket-84/ticket.json: record carries 1 unrecognised field(s): ticket
        [nominal]         discovery                 :: kind: ticket
```

Then edited **only** `kinds.json` (sha256 `1BC354E6…` → `29E4213F…`; `store.go` still 0 ticket keys) and restarted:

```
STEP 2  [needs-attention] record-unrecognised-fields :: ticket-84/ticket.json: record carries 1 unrecognised field(s): ticket
```

The `discovery` disappeared; the unrecognised-field condition persisted. That is genuinely *"I watched the vocabulary entry do its job."* The claim holds.

## CONSTITUTION CHECK

- **Law 1 (no confident falsehood)** — ❌ **VIOLATED twice.** R1-M1 (`showing last good` for a lane that never ran) and R1-M2 (`not yet polled` beside rendered tickets). Also R1-M4 (a false claim in the adapter's own contract).
- **Law 4 (nothing lost silently)** — ✅ **Honored.** Delete-on-departure is committed, not silent; faults reach `_faults.log` and are read at `goodnight/SKILL.md:90`; quarantine conditions fire for malformed/future-dated heartbeats (measured, S5/S8).
- **Law 6 (one owner)** — ✅ **Honored.** Seal reuses `Get-Sha256Hex` (`Sync-Freight.ps1:70,103`), never a second hashing rule. **No second latest-wins rule**: `computeFreightFreshness`'s max-by-`at` (`poll.go:542-545`) is the identical recency idiom `computeLiveFreshness` already uses (`poll.go:473-483`) for "newest observed record" — it is not fold's supersession/winner authority, and the fold has no `ticket` case at all (`assemble_test.go:960` asserts this non-vacuously). `recordDirs` single-sourced from `store.Record.Path`.
- **Law 7 (vocabularies as DATA)** — ✅ **Honored, and the second kind is JUSTIFIED, not a dodge.** I tested the premise rather than assuming it. `ticket-sync` answers a categorically different question ("did a run complete") from `ticket` ("what is queued"), and it is *necessary*: an empty store cannot otherwise prove a run happened, which is the ticket's central Law 1 requirement. Folding it into `kind=ticket` would require a sentinel subject to exclude it from `freightTickets`' `kind=="ticket"` switch (`assemble.go:252`) — the exact overloading the owner rejected for intent-vs-ticket. Both values are DATA in `schema/vocab/kinds.json`; `kind` carries no schema enum; an unrecognised kind renders a nominal `discovery` (measured, Adjudication 3).
- **Law 2 / D11** — ✅ registry-driven position flip; `TestLaneRegistryPin` still pins all five lanes.

# THE CAP'S OWN TRIGGER — the self-catch is REAL, and I measured it

The author reports finding a fix-created defect itself: the DR2-M1 correction falsified rev 2's own universal `presumed-lost` statement, because a minted-id loss record carries the shop handle in `subject`. **I did not take this on report.** I built the board from source at this base and ran both families through real HTTP in one store, one snapshot:

**Minted-id shape — the loss renders ON THE MEMBER ROW:**
```json
{"id":"train:lost","cars":[{"subject":"d87-lost-minted-r1","role":"car","state":"presumed-lost",
  "superseded":[{"kind":"dispatched","at":"…"}],"recordDir":"d87-lost-minted-r1"}, …],
 "declaredNotObserved":[]}
```
`assigned:true`. **Epoch 3, today, with no D8 at all.** Exactly as §5.2's new table claims.

**Runtime-id contrast — same store, same snapshot:**
```json
{"id":"train:lost-rt","cars":[],"declaredNotObserved":["d87-lost-rt-r1"]}
{"assigned":false,"state":"presumed-lost","subject":"zzzzzzzzzzzzzzzzz"}
```
**Zero board conditions.** Epoch 2, and the Law-3 cost §6 names is real and now measured: the board shows a member as *"declared, not yet observed"* while holding its death certificate under another name.

**Ruling: the self-catch is real, complete, and correctly scoped.** V14's three-direction rewrite follows from it, §5.7's narrowing of D8's inheritance obligation to the Claude family follows from it, and both are right. Rev 2 *would* have shipped a false universal statement; the author caught it inward before a reviewer saw it. **A defect the author caught and fixed before shipping does not fire the cap, and this one is exactly that.**

# RULINGS ON THE FIVE CLAIMS

### 1. Q8 / DR2-M2 — **the deletion is CORRECT, and declining my option (a) was the better call.**

**Ground (i) tested, not accepted.** The claim is that V7's precedence already neutralises shape A's mutation. Shape A's amendment ADDS `subject` to a member; under §5.5 a both-keys member resolves `task_id` first, so identity is unchanged. **True.** And rev 2's justification for V13 cited shape A while the clause actually forbade `task_id` rewriting — so rev 2's stated reason was indeed aimed at a threat V7 already holds. **The author is right that its own prior justification was false**, and saying so about your own work is the behaviour this shop wants.

**Is anything now unguarded?** I worked the cases:

| Operation | Detectable? | Outcome under rev 3 |
|---|---|---|
| Rewrite a member's `task_id` | **No** — byte-identical to remove-plus-add | Old handle's records unattributed + D7 disclosure; member renders unbound. Honest. |
| Shape A: add `subject` to a `task_id` member | Yes | Identity unchanged (V7 precedence). No effect. |
| Add `task_id` to a `subject`-only member | Yes | **Identity flips** — and V7 is the cause, not the guard. But outcome is honest: record unbinds, becomes unattributed, D7 discloses. |

**Nothing is unguarded.** Every path ends in disclosure, never in silence or falsehood — which is ground (ii), and ground (ii) is what actually carries the third row. That is my DR3-m3 below: a precision point, not a reversal.

**On my own option (a): I concede it, and the author's reasoning is better than mine.** Naming `New-TrainManifest.ps1` as the enforcer would put a CONTRACT rule inside ONE tool, leaving a hand-written manifest or any second producer unbound. That is Law 6 inverted, and re-homing the idea to §7 as an operator-facing *warning* with a trigger is the right disposition. **The reviewer offered the weaker of two options and the author took the stronger one with a better argument.**

**"Empty by construction" is a real answer, not an evaporation.** D1 mints identity once, D2 gives the manifest no runtime fact to mutate toward, V7 neutralises the historical attempt. An obligation whose honest answer is *"nothing is forbidden here, and here is why"* has landed.

### 2. DR2-M1 — **V15's gap is real, V15 closes it, and the three-population table is complete.**

**The gap is real.** V5 pins `task_id`↔`task_id`; V6 pins `subject`↔`subject`. The Copilot in-flight case is member-`task_id` ↔ record-`subject` — **neither vector reaches it**, and an entire runtime family runs through it every dispatch. Verified by reading both vector specs.

**V15's fixture is correct and its non-vacuity warning is sharp.** The author flags that V15 *"would pass today against a member carrying `subject` instead of `task_id`"* — correct: today's board joins subject↔subject, so a subject-fixture is green on arrival for the wrong reason. With a `task_id`-**only** member, `assemble.go:307` drops the member outright (the #85 bug), so V15 reds. **Red on arrival, honestly achievable.**

**The three-population enumeration is complete.** I hunted a fourth:

- Copilot **returned** records carry `subject` = handle *and* `task_id` = handle (`Produce-Artifact.ps1:199` resolves Copilot returned subjects from the envelope; `:358` writes `task_id`), so they bind by the **first** clause. Correctly excluded.
- Claude **dispatched** records traverse the second clause but bind to nothing — that is epoch 2, covered elsewhere. The table's column is *"which clause binds it"*, so a non-binding traversal is correctly out of scope, though "serves" is doing some work in the lead sentence.

**Closing Q4 with its defective basis KEPT VISIBLE is the right call**, and it is the showcase rule applied to the document's own history: *"a soft-spot question priced wrongly is worse than no question at all."* Quietly restating the basis would have erased the most instructive thing that happened in three rounds — that the section whose job is honest self-attack had itself become a route to a wrong decision.

### 3. The minors — **arithmetic verified, and declining to decide Q2's shape half is CORRECT.**

**§5.6 arithmetic checks exactly.** In-table (§0's six obligations): V1,V2,V11 / V3,V4 / V5,V6,V15 / V7 / V8,V9 / V13 = **12 distinct**. Listed separately as post-§0: V10, V12, V14, V16 = **4**. Total **16 = V1–V16**, no vector double-counted, none orphaned. **Five complete, one PARTIAL**, and the one uncovered obligation is named precisely: the intra-manifest member duplicate, routed to Q5. V12 correctly moved off the join row; V15 correctly takes that slot.

**Declining to decide Q2's shape half is correct, not a dodge.** It is a contract question about what the producer writes into the store — the reality-vs-spec doctrine's territory, and D8 is already owner-gated. Decisively: **the author states a preference (option iii) rather than hiding behind neutrality.** That is escalate-without-pre-answering done properly, which is the exact discipline the #33 scar exists to enforce. One option is ambiguously worded — DR3-m4 below.

### 4. Q7 — **the carry survived into BOTH places. Confirmed.**

# FINDINGS — 0 Major, 4 Minor

**DR3-m1 — §5.2's minted-id row cites dispatched-record code for a `presumed-lost` claim, and says "works today" of a record kind nothing mints.** §5.2:421-424. The cited `Produce-Artifact.ps1:187-190` sets the **dispatched** record's subject; it says nothing about `presumed-lost`. And **no component in this repo mints `presumed-lost`** — `grep` across `scripts/` and `board/` finds only readers (`Detect-Dispatches.ps1:163-164`, `fold/algorithm.go:10,139`, `assemble.go:424`, `server/poll.go`), with 0 records in the store. *The conclusion is nonetheless correct and I measured it* (above). The real basis is that the fold groups by subject with `presumed-lost` superseding `dispatched`, which **forces** the shared subject — my probe exercised exactly that (`superseded:[{kind:"dispatched"}]`). Fix: cite the fold's subject-grouping as the basis, and soften *"works today"* to *"binds the moment such a record exists."*

**DR3-m2 — the `presumed-lost` inheritance obligation has no owner, because nothing mints `presumed-lost`.** §5.7:747-753 makes it *"an explicit D8 obligation"* that a Claude-family loss record carries its dispatched sibling's handle. But D8 is a change to `scripts/Produce-Artifact.ps1`, which writes `dispatched` and `returned` only; the loss-record minter is a different, **unbuilt** component (`schema/vectors/valid-presumed-lost.json`'s `basis.by` reads `watchdog-tier1`), and §8 has no row for it. An obligation assigned to software that does not exist and no ticket owns is the carrier rule's own shape. **Stated explicitly because it bears on the cap: this is NOT fix-created — it entered at rev 2 via DR1-m4's fold and rev 3 only narrowed it per-family.** Fix: one §2c row or one §8 row.

**DR3-m3 — the V13-deletion's ground (i) holds in one direction only; ground (ii) is what carries the other.** §5.6:666-672. V7's precedence neutralises *adding `subject`* to a `task_id` member — but the reverse addition, `task_id` added to a `subject`-only member, **flips** the resolved identity, with V7's precedence as the cause rather than the guard. D4 (§4) makes a subject-only member schema-legal; only D3, a design rule, discourages one. **The deletion remains correct** — ground (ii)'s honest-outcome argument covers that direction fully. Q8 asks *"is anything now unguarded"*, and the answer deserves to name which ground does the work in each direction rather than resting on one that holds one way.

**DR3-m4 — Q2's option (ii) is ambiguous between rejecting the FIELD and rejecting the RECORD, and the record reading is an unpriced Law 4 hazard.** §10:979-982: *"a shape rule on the record schema … so a non-handle is **rejected at write time**."* If that means the record is rejected, `Produce-Artifact.ps1`'s validation path raises and **no dispatch record is written** — a dispatch that happened and is invisible, which is the #32 killed-session class this design elsewhere treats as the manifest's whole purpose. If it means only the field, it collapses into option (iii). The author's stated preference (iii) is the safe one, so the design's instinct is right — but the owner is being asked to choose among options one of which is readable two ways. Fix: one disambiguating clause, and if record-rejection is meant, price the Law 4 cost.

### R1-M4 (Law 1) — fixed; chosen remedy: **correct the header**

`scripts/Sync-Freight.ps1`'s "FAILURE SURFACES AS STALENESS" section no longer claims "writes NOTHING." It now states the true, measured contract: writes are not atomic as a set; a mid-run failure leaves earlier writes on disk; the ticket-sync heartbeat is the one thing that never appears after a failure; and R1-M2's board-side detection (not an atomic-write guarantee) is what keeps a partial failure from ever rendering silently. **Reasoning for choosing "correct the header" over "make writes atomic":** true atomicity would require restructuring the store layout (a staged-directory swap), which changes `recordDir`/`assemble.recordDirBySubject` conventions other lanes already depend on — a structural change, not a fix-cycle-scope one, and R1-M2 already closes the actual defect (a silent contradiction on the render surface) from the reader's side. The reviewer's own Adjudication 1 explicitly offered this as one of two acceptable remedies ("fix the premise ... or have the board detect and say so — not the posture"), and I did both: corrected the false premise AND kept R1-M2's detection.

New red-first regression (`SyncFreight.Tests.ps1`) reaches a genuine mid-run failure through the script's real public interface — a pre-existing file blocks the second of two ticket directory creations — never a code injection:
```
REGRESSION (R1-M4): the earlier write in the loop must survive a LATER failure ...
```
(pinned green; ticket-1 survives, `ticket-sync.json` never appears, `_faults.log` gets the entry).

## MAJOR FINDINGS

### R2-M1 — The R1-M1 fix falsified its own state-ledger rows, and the ledger was not trued in the same commit

`CLAUDE.md` Living contracts is unambiguous: *"Any commit that adds or changes mutable service state updates the state ledger in the same commit… **Reviewers reject state-touching diffs that leave the ledger stale.**"* This commit changed the **write condition** of both ledgered maps and left all three claims describing the old unconditional behaviour.

Code (`board/server/poll.go`):
- `:400` `if freightFreshnessVal.Kind != "never-polled" { s.lastGoodAsOf["freight"] = newLastGood }`
- `:446` `if freightFreshnessVal.Kind != "never-polled" { s.lastGoodLaneData[spec.ID] = assembled.Freight }`

Document (`docs/contracts/state-ledger.md`), poll-success column, unchanged:
- **`:115`** — `lastGoodAsOf["live"|"freight"]` → **"UPDATED (both keys, same poll's `now`)"**. False whenever freight is never-polled.
- **`:116`** — `lastGoodLaneData[laneID]` → **"UPDATED per live lane ID (dispatches/gates/trains/freight, #84)"**. False for freight when never-polled.
- **`:136-139`** — the Question-1 amendment states: *"Lifecycle behaviour (restart/poll-success/scan-failure/reconnect) is **IDENTICAL in shape** to the existing `"live"` key's row… so **no new lifecycle column values were needed**."* This is now affirmatively wrong — divergence on poll-success **is the entire mechanism of the R1-M1 fix**.

`grep -n "never-polled" docs/contracts/state-ledger.md` returns **only** the unrelated `lastGoodSnapshot` row at `:111`. The gating condition appears nowhere in the ledger.

This is not a nitpick about tone: the ledger's own house style states conditionals explicitly (the `:116` row already spells out *"on the FIRST-EVER poll failure (empty map, nothing to retain) `lane.Data` stays `nil`"*). A reader auditing against `:115` would conclude freight's `lastGoodAsOf` always advances on a successful scan, and would then be unable to explain the very behaviour I measured in probe A. That is the lying-canary harm the rule exists to prevent.

### R2-M2 — `poll.go`'s state-ledger citation drifted to the table header, caused by this commit — the second drift of this same citation

`board/server/poll.go:123` cites `docs/contracts/state-ledger.md:108` for the `lastGoodLaneData` row, and its own text records that it has drifted before: *"#52 C51R-4: corrected from :102, which is the `seq` row, not this field's row"*.

Measured:
- At **`bc552df`**: `state-ledger.md:108` = the `lastGoodLaneData[laneID]` row. **Correct** (I verified and credited this in round 1).
- This commit inserted **8 content lines** (the R1-m6 "Amended…" block) *above* line 108.
- At **`baf109e`**: line 108 = the **table header** `| Field (owner class) | Process restart | Poll cycle (success) | …`; the real row moved to **`:116`**. 108 + 8 = 116, exactly.

Why the suite is green: `board/web/js/lanes.js:16-21` — a comment **this car edited for R1-m1** — states the reason verbatim: *"a line-number citation into a file this SAME train's commit still edits drifts on every later insertion, and **the #65 gate does not check in-range shifts**."* Line 108 still exists, so the citation resolver resolves it; it simply points at the wrong content. The car read that warning, edited around it, and then reproduced the exact defect it describes.

`docs/templates/car-brief.md:122-123`: *"Open every file:line the diff's docs cite and confirm the citation is true. A stale document or a dead citation is a MAJOR finding."* Low blast radius (it lands on the header of the correct table), but it is the third instance of one recurring defect. **The fix that ends it is the symbol form `lanes.js` already uses**, not `:116`.

# FINDINGS

## **A1-M1 (Major) — PR-8's headline figure is false by the design's own stated metric, in seven places.**

§2b PR-8 (line 327) states the metric precisely: *"for every runtime `subject`, **how many distinct shop handles (`task_id`s on its `returned` records)** does it serve?"* — then answers *"**43 of 109** subjects serve MORE THAN ONE member."*

**Measured by me over the same store, by that exact definition: 26, not 43.**

```
A) distinct task_id count GT 1        : 26      &lt;- the stated metric
B) returned-record count GT 1         : 43      &lt;- what 43 actually counts
```

**The 17-subject gap is the opposite of the claim.** Those subjects hold **35 returned records between them, 0 carrying `task_id`** — pre-#47 fossils predating the envelope echo. They serve **zero identified members**, not "more than one". Six of the 17 are `train:board-v0` members from the legacy corpus I measured in round 1.

**Seven locations:** lines 39 (header), 327 (PR-8), 421 (D10's reason column), 995 (*"wrong on more than a third of this shop's real corpus"* — 26/109 is 24%, about a quarter), 1000 (§5.8a), 1123 (§9b), 1328 (Q9).

**Everything else in PR-8 is exact, and I want that on the record separately:** 109 subjects ✓; **76 distinct handles ✓** (unaffected, precisely because the 17 contribute none); max FIVE rounds ✓; **every multi-group has exactly one `dispatched`, 0 exceptions ✓**; and all three named exemplars exact — `ab2bd332be0eebc2c` = `tooling-65-car-r1`…`-r5` **all `SUCCESS`** ✓, `af5f0b4f753a8f9d7` = `tooling-50-32-car-r1`…`-r5` ✓, `a353ad2db7e6ad627` = `view-75-review-r1/r2/r3` ✓.

**D10 survives with a wide margin** — 26 subjects and 76 handles is ample basis, and I confirmed the mechanism directly (below). This is a correction, not a redesign.

**Class, and it is why this is Major rather than Minor:** this is DR1-M1's exact shape at the same rung — a real number answering a different question than its sentence asks — inside the section whose own opening promises *"every behavioural claim in this document is traceable to an observation rather than a reading."* The author diagnosed DR1-M1's root cause as *"a reading dressed as a measurement"*; this is one measurement dressed as another.

**Constructive: the honest statement is stronger than the false one.** *"26 subjects are MEASURED to serve more than one member; a further 17 have multiple returned records carrying no handle at all, so what they served is **unknowable**."* Unknowable provenance is an even worse thing to dereference than shared provenance — the correction reinforces D10.

## **A1-M2 (Major) — PR-8 makes an unresolved ambiguity in D5 outcome-determining, and one reading reproduces this design's own founding defect on a quarter of the store.**

§5.5 states the join over "**a record**". But a bound member's liveness, outcome and superseded history come from a **fold entry** — `board/assemble/assemble.go:110` (`dispatchBySubject[m.Subject]`, built from `fold.Output`) — and the fold keeps **exactly one entry per subject** (`board/fold/algorithm.go:154-163`, `foldDispatchSubject` returning one `DispatchEntry` per subject).

**Measured.** I wrote the real `ab2bd332be0eebc2c` shape into a scratch store — 1 `dispatched` + 5 `returned`, distinct handles, outcomes REJECT×4 then APPROVE — and ran the real board:

```
RECORDS WRITTEN: 6 under ONE subject
DISPATCH ROWS RENDERED: 1
  subject=ab2bd332be0eebc2c state=returned outcome=APPROVE taskId=tooling-65-car-r5 superseded=5
```

**Under fold-entry semantics, only `-r5` can bind.** Members `-r1`…`-r4` render *"declared, no departure bound"* **while their returned records sit in the store carrying their handles and their outcomes** — and **no condition is raised**, because the single fold entry IS assigned, to `-r5`. That is precisely PR-1's Law 1 shape — the board asserting members have not departed while holding their returned records — arriving through the fold collapse instead of through shape B, on the 26 subjects and 76 handles PR-8 just measured.

Under raw-record semantics all five bind, but then liveness and superseded history must come from somewhere the fold does not provide per member, which the design also does not specify.

**The amendment draws exactly one conclusion from PR-8 — D10, about the pointer — and never asks the same question of D5, its central mechanism.** §5.8a's own sentence applies verbatim to the binding: *"the collapse happens in the fold before any consumer sees it."* A `grep` for `fold winner` / `fold entry` / `raw record` finds the collapse addressed only for the pointer (line 1013) and for manifest supersession (line 1024).

**In scope:** PR-8 is new work introduced by this amendment, and this is its unexamined implication. The underlying fact is the one the ruling names as *"the same fact behind #89"*. **Likely a small fix:** say whether §5.5's join runs over raw records or fold entries; if fold entries, add a §6 failure row and a vector for the members a resumed agent served that the fold cannot surface.

## **A1-m1 (Minor) — a FOURTH surviving D2-strict claim, undisclosed. The sweep reports three.**

**P3, lines 177-180:** *"A structured identity … would let a member carry the binding inline. That is precisely the shape P5 kills: **it puts a runtime fact inside a plan record**, and it re-imports mutation, supersession-of-identity and ordering."*

D2′ now expressly permits a runtime fact inside a plan record, so P3's first stated ground is superseded — **the same shape as disclosed survivor (c)** (V13's *"D2 gives the manifest no runtime fact to mutate toward"*), annotated nowhere. P3's *conclusion* survives on independent grounds (D1/D3 keep identity one string; D10 keeps the pointer out of identity), so harm is bounded — but the sweep is presented as complete at three and it is four. One annotation fixes it.

*Sweep otherwise verified complete:* I grepped independently for D2-strict phrasings and the only other hits are D2 itself (correctly struck, line 419) and the two disclosed survivors.

# RULINGS ON THE FIVE

**1. D10 — CORRECT, not over-strict, adequately specified. Say it plainly, as asked.** I tested the reasoning against the real fold rather than accepting it, and the probe above is the confirmation: five rounds under one runtime id collapse to **one** row carrying round 5's outcome. Had five members pointed at that subject and the board dereferenced, all five would have rendered round 5 — four rows in five false, and entirely plausible-looking. The reasoning is exactly right, including *"no amount of care in the implementation removes it, because the collapse happens in the fold."* **Not over-strict:** conditional dereferencing (only when 1:1) would require scanning every manifest at render time and would silently flip unsafe the moment a member is added later. **Not under-specified:** §5.8a names the permitted use — a link to the record *directory* — which stays honest under many-to-one because the directory shows all rounds rather than one false row. *Bonus for the format car: `af5f0b4f753a8f9d7` is the better V19 exemplar than the one cited — its five rounds carry MIXED outcomes (`done-with-findings` at r2, `done` elsewhere), so dereferencing there mislabels a findings round as clean.*

**2. The measurement — re-derived above.** Six of seven figures exact; the headline is A1-M1.

**3. The field spec (§5.8) — precise enough for a format car, and "raw and byte-verbatim" IS testable.** Placement, type, optionality, and three explicit NOs (not identity, not a join key, not a liveness source) each map to a vector. Byte-verbatim has a testable meaning because V18 pins `observed_subject` == the record's `subject` byte-for-byte, with the withdrawn composite as the red half. The name reasoning is sound — carrying the ruling's own vocabulary rather than renaming underneath it, with `ran_as` and `runtime_subject` named and declined for stated reasons.

**4. The falsification sweep — three disclosed survivors all real and honestly handled; a fourth exists (A1-m1).** P4's treatment is exemplary: *"THE ANTECEDENT CAME TRUE AND THE CONSEQUENT DID NOT"*, recorded as a miss rather than deleted, *"because a premise table whose wrong forecasts vanish teaches nothing about how to forecast."* P5's four-claim status table correctly separates the surviving narrow form from the over-reach, and naming *"sufficiency is not exclusivity"* is the right diagnosis of its own error.

**5. The five vectors — writable, and V18's red half genuinely reds.** V18's red half is a pointer carrying `-r2`; no record subject ever carries a suffix, so a byte-verbatim comparison fails — and it usefully traps anyone re-proposing the withdrawn composite. V19 is the vector that protects the ruling and is writable (I built essentially its fixture). V20's two directions are both record-checkable. V21's NO-BACKFILL pin is right. V17 folds my Q5 ruling as ruled. **Arithmetic verified: 13 in-table (V1,V2,V11 / V3,V4 / V5,V6,V15 / V7 / V8,V9,V17 / V13) + 8 post-§0 (V10,V12,V14,V16,V18,V19,V20,V21) = 21 = V1–V21, no overlap, none orphaned.**

**Also confirmed honest:** the §9b round-1 disposition row appends a `[REV-4 NOTE]` rather than rewriting — it preserves what rev 1 recorded, names the superseding decision, and quotes the exact sentence the owner overruled with the reasoning. The header's cap paragraph now reads *"Rev 3 was APPROVED (0 Major, 4 Minor; the round-2 cap did NOT fire) and merged to `dev` at `8bcaf10`"* — accurate to my verdict. **All four DR3 Minors folded**, DR3-m2 taking *both* rows I offered plus carrying my not-fix-created note forward, and DR3-m4 splitting option (ii) into (ii-a)/(ii-b) with (ii-b) priced as manufacturing an invisible dispatch and **recommended for outright rejection** rather than quietly dropped.
